# Lightroom parity checklist

> **Last reviewed:** 2026-10-12 · **Target:** Adobe Lightroom Classic 15.5 / Lightroom 9.6

The measured, row-by-row checklist: one row per Lightroom feature, menu item and shortcut, what we
have and what is missing. It answers *does the feature exist?* (feature breadth). Whether it is good
enough to replace Lightroom is assessed in [target-app-parity.md](target-app-parity.md), and the work
list is [gaps.md](gaps.md). Whoever lands a feature updates its row in the same commit.

The rows are maintained by hand; the summary block is generated (`cargo xtask parity --write`), and
`cargo xtask parity` (part of `cargo xtask ci`) checks that every command id, control id and path
cited here still exists. Formerly docs/parity.md.

- **Ids** are described in `plan/lightroom-classic/` (committed, our own words): `LR-…` = feature catalog,
  `MENU-…` = menu items, `KEY-…` / `KEYC-…` = keyboard shortcuts (desktop / Classic), `LRC-…` = Classic extras,
  `IMM-…` = Immich integration (`plan/immich.md`). Ids are stable; never renumber.
- **Tier** as in the catalog: **P0** core, **P1** important parity, **P2** later / AI / niche, **OOS** out of scope.
- **Status**: ✅ done (usable end to end; polish may be noted) · 🟡 partial (note says what is missing, or the status
  is unverified) · ⬜ missing · 🚫 out of scope / not applicable.
- **Evidence**: `cmd:<id>` = command id (engine `command_specs()` or UI `UI_COMMANDS`), `ctl:<id>` = develop control
  id (`app-cli controls`), plus source files. A trailing `*` matches a prefix (`ctl:mixer.*`).
- Feature names and notes are our own words. No Adobe text, screenshots or assets belong here.

## Top gaps and shortcut notes

Moved on 2026-10-10: the ranked gaps are in [gaps.md](gaps.md); shortcut conflicts, deliberate
differences and missing bindings are in [ui-parity.md](ui-parity.md#shortcuts).

**Chromaforge's own gaps** (Classic modules, Immich):

Ordered by user impact, then tier, then effort. The checklist above counts features that *exist*; these are the gaps
that decide whether a photographer can switch (see the honest assessment in [ROADMAP.md](../ROADMAP.md#where-we-stand)).
Take the first one nobody is working on.

1. **LR-PROF-CAMERACOLOR** (P0): our own camera colour calibration. Sony ARW, Nikon NEF, Panasonic RW2 and Fujifilm RAF now get a guarded file-local fit to their own embedded JPEG (colour matrix + hue/saturation/value table + tone and chroma curves, relative WB; per-model profiles pooled from many photos via `app-cli calibrate` for ARW, NEF and RAF; ARW: 7 of 10 public samples accepted, mean ΔE vs the camera JPEG 17–26 → 3–10; NEF: 13 of 13 decodable samples from 6 bodies accepted, ΔE 13–46 → 3–8, one mixed-light scene 13 → 12; RW2: 140 of 174 public samples accepted, from 93 of the 114 bodies with a preview, median ΔE 5.0; RAF: 23 of 24 references accepted with bundled X-H2S/X-T4 profiles; 150 withheld-file tests improve mean ΔE slightly over per-file fitting); measured calibration and fidelity remain missing. Other non-DNG raws and rejected fits still use a neutral matrix. Expand validated preview fitting and use matrices the files carry themselves; never Adobe data.
2. **LR-IMP-FORMATS** (P0): CR3 lossless Bayer and version 0x100/0x200 C-RAW now decodes (M50/R100/R8 full sensor exact). Remaining unverified CRX variants, compressed RAF, Nikon
   Canon sRAW (AVIF decodes since 2026-10-13; HEIC decodes behind the codecs' non-default `heif` feature:
   the optional `lcc-heif` crate on heic-rs, the same decoder and policy as PhotoCraft). Clean-room, from prose descriptions only (see
   `crates/raw/src/vendor/nefc.rs` for how compressed NEF was done). Until decoded, such photos are `preview_only`.
3. **LR-IMP-CAMERA-COVERAGE** (P0): per-model verification; grow the CC0 corpus and fix per-model bugs (like the CR2
   colour-filter layout, fixed in #85 by reading the file's own tag).
4. **LR-BEHAV-RENDER-FIDELITY** (P1): a side-by-side fidelity suite against Lightroom renders (kept local in `plan/`),
   then tune tone, highlights, texture/clarity/dehaze, NR and sharpening against it. A retune that changes how existing
   settings render ships as a new process version, so existing edits keep their look (`docs/process-versions.md`).
5. **LR-VIEW-ZOOM** (P0): the loupe, Before/After, overlays, soft proofing, Compare and Reference are cached tile
   grids, slider drags at 1:1 draft one stage-cached window, and 1:1 equals export on 87 corpus raws; still to do:
   checking sharpness and the wide-stage margin by eye on real raws, and a tick under 16 ms at 1:1 on a 2800 px canvas.
6. **LR-EDIT-OPTICS-PROFILE** (P1): a lens-profile database of our own (corrections embedded in DNG files and the
   distortion correction Panasonic / Leica cameras record in RW2 / RWL files work today; issue #256).
7. **AI masks and Enhance** (LR-MASK-SUBJECT / SKY / PEOPLE / OBJECTS, LR-EDIT-DETAIL-DENOISE, SUPERRES, LENSBLUR):
   Object and Describe masks run SAM 3 in pure Rust (`crates/segment`); the weights (SAM License) are never bundled:
   the app offers a consented, verified download, but **upstream's CDN mirrors are not configured yet** (the
   built-in list is empty, so today users need their own mirror or a manual install). Subject / Sky / People could
   use the same model with fixed prompts; denoise / super-resolution models remain a maintainer decision.
8. **Classic shell** (KEYC-MODULES, LRC-SHELL-*, LRC-CAT-*): module picker, panel system and Classic keymap (the catalog
   now scales to 500k–1M photos, LRC-CAT-SCALE). Then **Immich**: connect, link, import and external libraries exist (Phase 1, see
   [immich.md](immich.md)); two-way sync, people, smart search and publish now exist (Phase 4); keychains on macOS/Windows, sync at scale, album renames and the sync stream remain.
9. **HDR** (Q. HDR, LR-EXP-HDR), **video** (R. Video). The **Classic output modules** (Map view, Book, Slideshow,
   Print, Web, publish) exist in Chromaforge (LRC-MAP-*, LRC-BOOK-*, LRC-SS-*, LRC-WEB-*, LRC-PRINT-*); what remains there:
   slideshow sound output and video export, the slideshow backdrop image in playback, publish grid sections.

Recognition runtime gap closed (LR-PREF-PEOPLE): the pinned SFace and AuraFace models now run on the shared pure-Rust
CPU engine and agree with an independent reference runtime on public crops. Live-face threshold calibration, small-face
coverage and separated clusters remain open; see [faces.md](faces.md).

## Shortcuts: conflicts and missing bindings

Compared `plan/lightroom/06-shortcuts.md` (desktop part) with our bindings: command specs in `crates/engine/src/cmd/`,
`UI_COMMANDS` in `crates/ui-egui/src/menus.rs` and secondary bindings (`ALIASES`) in `crates/ui-egui/src/shortcuts.rs`.
`no_conflicting_bindings` (same file) fails when one key fires two actions.

**Fixed (M16.1):** added Lightroom-desktop keys as secondary bindings for existing commands — ⌘D Select None, ⇧E
Export dialog, Space Toggle zoom, ⇧M Create Version, ⇧X Reject + advance, ⇧U Unflag + advance. `⇧Y` fired both
Before/After Split and the History panel; History lost the binding. `W` and `⇧⌘I` also fired the engine command
under the UI command that wraps it (a no-op error); the UI command now wins.

**Deliberate differences (our key → Lightroom desktop key)** — each is a conflict with another binding we have:

| Action | Ours | Lightroom desktop | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-20 | minor | LR-VID-PLAY, LRC-SS-MUSIC: default macOS / Windows builds play sound through cpal (CoreAudio / WASAPI); `audio-out` is now an opt-in for other targets; status unchanged (🟡) |
| Pick flag | P | Z | Z = toggle zoom (Classic convention); P is the Classic pick key |
| Photos panel (left) | ⌘⇧L | P | P = pick |
| Expand/collapse edit sections | ⌘⌥1–5 | ⌘1–6 | ⌘0 = Zoom to Fit, ⌘1 = Zoom 100 %; Geometry lives in the Crop panel |
| Histogram | ⌘⇧H | ⌘0 | ⌘0 = Zoom to Fit |
| Square Grid | ⇧G | (none; ⇧G = Guided Upright) | Guided Upright is a button in the Crop panel |
| Export dialog | ⌘⇧E (+ ⇧E) | ⇧E | ⌘⇧E is "Edit in Photoshop" there; no external-editor command yet |
| Crop overlay cycle | ⇧O | O | O = mask overlay; ⇧O (mask colour / overlay orientation) unused otherwise |
| Create Version | ⌘⇧S (+ ⇧M) | ⇧M (Windows: Ctrl+⇧S) | — |
| Select None | ⌘⇧A (+ ⌘D) | ⌘D | — |

**Library culling (KEYC-RATING):** `0–5`, `6–9` and `P/X/U` reach egui on macOS even when displayed in the native menu (adapted from PR #261; issue #283). `Shift+0–9` applies and advances once; `Shift+P` picks and advances in Photo Grid / Square Grid and retains Presets elsewhere. See [library shortcuts](library-shortcuts.md) for the verified Classic mapping and regression coverage. Rating `[` / `]` (grids), \` flag toggle, `\` filter bar and ⌘L Library ▸ Filters ▸ Enable Filters (`library.toggleFilters`, keeps the filter while off) are bound too (G28).

**Still missing / broken:**
- No command yet: F1 help (verify the rest of the old list: full screen, settings, stacks,
  visualize spots and merges have commands now).
- `H` opens Remove; Lightroom also uses it (Classic) to hide pins — pins toggle from View → Show Mask Pins.
- ⌘M / ⌘H / ⌘Q / ⌘W rely on the platform window defaults (unverified).

## A. Import (IMP)

| Id | Feature | Tier | Status | Evidence | Missing / notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LR-HDR-EDIT | HDR editing | P2 | ✅ | `cmd:develop.hdr`, `crates/pipeline/src/tone.rs` (`ToneMap::hdr`), `crates/pipeline/src/tests_hdr.rs`, `crates/gpu/src/wgsl/finish.wgsl` | HDR tone map (SDR curve through the midtones, highlights up to the headroom limit) and float HDR render, on the GPU too (linear float output up to the peak, the CPU as oracle: `hdr_renders_match` in `crates/gpu/tests/it/equivalence.rs`; gap 14); shown on HDR displays in every view (LR-VIEW-HDR-DISPLAY); `crs:HDREditMode`, `HDRMaxValue`, `SDRBlend` and the SDR sliders are read from XMP |
| LR-IMP-ADD-DIALOG | Add photos/folders | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:dialog.import`, `cmd:file.addPhotos`, `cmd:library.importPreview`, `cmd:library.import` (`mode`, `album`, `albumName`, `preset`, `keywords`), `crates/ui-egui/src/import.rs` | review dialog (Import Photos): the scanned source (a scanned folder is not added to Local), candidate grid with thumbnails and checkboxes (duplicates by path/content marked and unchecked; with several file types, a toggle per type checks or unchecks all of its files, e.g. the raws without the JPEGs beside them, issue #344) filling a resizable window (issue #337), transfer mode (add in place / copy / move, each explained), album (existing/new), preset, keywords; the folder scan runs on a background thread with a progress window and Cancel (a NAS folder no longer freezes the window); the import itself (probing, sidecars, copying / moving files) runs on a worker thread and its batches join the library between frames, with a progress window and Cancel; one undo step. Drag-and-drop goes through the same background import. File dialogs list the importable extensions (`lcc_engine::import::EXTENSIONS`) in any letter case on Linux, where GTK and the desktop portal match globs case-sensitively (`apps/app/src/dialog_filter.rs`, issue #342; 0.4.0 hid `.JPG` / `.NEF`, issue #705). The folder scan, Browse Folder, drag-and-drop and the sidecar lookup match extensions in any case (`crates/engine/src/tests_import.rs`); a rating, reject flag and colour label embedded in the file (XMP / EXIF) are kept on import, a sidecar still winning (`crates/engine/src/tests_import.rs`); the review (and `library.importPreview`: `truncated`, `skipped {unreadable, unsupported}`) says when its folder scan stopped at the walk limit, could not read folders or skipped files that are not a supported photo type (XMP sidecars not counted; upstream #2944) |
| 2026-10-12 | minor | LR-IMP-FORMATS: Mamiya MEF decodes from sensor data and is imported (`vendor/mef.rs`) |
| LR-IMP-DRAGDROP | Drop files/folders to import | P0 | ✅ | `crates/ui-egui/src/lib.rs` (dropped files → the import review), `crates/ui-egui/src/drop_overlay.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/tests_drop_import.rs` | while files are dragged over the window a veil and a card say what dropping does (import N items, review a folder for import, import N presets, open the face model; a note to close the open dialog first when one is open); dropped photo files and folders open the import review as File ▸ Import does, or, with Settings ▸ Import ▸ Import dropped photos without review (off by default; from OneSlips), are added at once on a worker thread with the library's import settings (the web build imports both at once); with no library open on the desktop (the launch list, a library that couldn't open) photos wait for the library opened and presets are refused, and the card says so; a drop while a dialog is open (also in the web build) or while an import is running is refused with a toast, and the card says so; a finished scan waits for an open dialog to close before its review opens; the control channel's `ui.dropFiles` hovers or drops files for scripts; dropping onto a specific album not supported |
| LR-IMP-DUPES | Skip duplicates by content | P1 | ✅ | `crates/engine/src/import.rs`, `crates/engine/src/tests_import.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/import.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/headless.rs` | Only library records (including Recently Deleted) count as existing content; a copy merely browsed in Local does not block importing another path. A browsed file at the same path is offered by the scan (not "In library") and promoted with its edits, unless a library match or an earlier promotion already has its content; background import batches deduplicate promoted files too. The review offers such a file only when adding in place (copy and move would add it where it is), and notes that keywords and presets apply to new files only. |
| LR-IMP-RECIPE | Batch edits during import | P2 | ✅ | `cmd:library.import` (`recipe`), `cmd:preset.saveRecipe`, `crates/ui-egui/src/import.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/import_screen.rs` | an optional Batch edits section in the import review: sliders for everyday adjustments (Light, Vibrance and Saturation, Clarity, Texture and Dehaze, Sharpening, Grain, Vignette) whose values are set on every photo the import adds, after its XMP sidecar, camera default and the chosen preset, as one extra History step (`Import adjustments`) after the preset's own; a photo the library already has (a duplicate, one restored from Recently Deleted, one joined from a browsed folder) is not changed; a summary beside the section counts the adjustments, it does not list them; Save as Preset stores only the settings the recipe names (`preset.saveRecipe`) so it can be reused from the Preset list; the recipe lives only in the dialog until the import runs, so Cancel changes neither the catalog nor the preset collections. The command takes any plain develop control, not only the ones the dialog offers, and refuses white balance, geometry and crop controls, per-photo controls and unknown ids before anything is imported. Not a second editor: white balance is not offered (a temperature means something else on a raw than on a rendered photo, so the command refuses it too), and neither are crop, masks, curve or point colour; a slider left at its default names nothing, so setting a control to its default for the whole batch is only possible through the command |
| LR-IMP-DEVICE | Import from camera/card | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:library.devices`, `cmd:file.addFromDevice`, `crates/engine/src/devices.rs` | mounted volumes with a DCIM folder (macOS /Volumes, Linux /media and /run/media, Windows drive letters); File → Import from Device → the import review, copying into the library by default; menus serve the last scan and rescan in the background (never blocking a frame; hot-plugs repaint); copies are verified (a new file, synced, checked against the content hash the scan computed — byte compare with the source when there is none, issue #134; a bad copy is removed and reported as failed, never counted as imported; taken names get -1, -2…, never replaced — issue #96, `crates/engine/src/import_move.rs`); no PTP/MTP (cameras that don't mount as a disk); Settings → Import → Cameras and Cards can open the import review when a camera or card is connected (off by default; a card that was already in doesn't ask, and an open dialog or running import is finished first; `crates/ui-egui/src/import.rs`) |
| LR-IMP-AUTO | Watched-folder auto import | P2 | ✅ | `cmd:library.autoImport`, `cmd:library.autoImportScan`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/settings.rs` | Settings ▸ Import ▸ Auto Import: a watched folder whose new photos are added (in place or copied) once complete, into an optional album; scanned every 3 s; Include subfolders (bounded walk, hidden and library folders skipped), file naming template for copies, develop preset, metadata preset and keywords for arriving photos (`library.autoImport` `subfolders`/`rename`/`preset`/`metadataPreset`/`keywords`; upstream #1117) |
| LR-IMP-PRESET | Preset on import | P2 | ✅ | `cmd:library.import` (`preset`) | chosen in the import review; one History entry |
| LR-IMP-RAWDEFAULT | Raw defaults | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:library.preferences`, `cmd:library.setImportDefault`, `crates/engine/src/import.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/settings.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/edit.rs` | built-in default / a preset / per camera (make + model); non-raw default too; the preset look counts as unedited and Reset returns to it; Develop ▸ Optics says what new photos start from and saves the current Optics settings as that preset in one action, under a name the user chooses (several named defaults live side by side — one per camera, say) — the camera's own entry when per-camera defaults are on (issue #148) |
| LR-IMP-MIGRATE | Migrate other catalogs | OOS | 🚫 | | |
| LR-IMP-PROFILES | Import profiles & presets | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:file.importPresets`, `cmd:preset.import`, `cmd:profile.import`, `crates/engine/src/preset_import.rs`, `crates/engine/src/preset_luminar.rs` | presets: .lcpreset, XMP, classic .lrtemplate (local adjustment templates become mask effects, LR-MASK-EFFECTS; metadata and export templates, XMP metadata templates, `.lcmeta` and `.lcexport` become metadata / export presets, LRC-LIB-METADATA, LR-EXP-DIALOG), photos carrying edits ("DNG presets"), Luminar looks (.lmp, .mplumpack collections; sliders with a counterpart), .zip bundles, folders (folder → group), drag & drop (an `.xmp` dropped together with its photo is that photo's sidecar and is not imported as a preset, issue #936); masks carried over; unmapped settings reported (fields that only describe the preset or photo, e.g. `Cluster`, `SortName`, `SupportsAmount2`, the as-shot white, empty Point Color slots, are not); profiles: .cube 3D LUTs (one toast reports both halves; a LUT that fails is named with its reason); a preset that names a profile (`crs:Look`) applies the imported profile of that name, imported before or after it, and Lightroom's basic profiles map to ours (`crates/engine/src/profile_refs.rs`, issue #1001); Adobe profile formats deliberately unsupported (so `.xmp` creative profiles' colour tables aren't read) |
| LR-IMP-LOCAL | Work on files in place | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:library.browse`, `cmd:photo.addToLibrary`, `cmd:library.import` (mode add), `cmd:local.addRoot`, `cmd:local.hide`, `cmd:local.restoreHidden`, `crates/engine/src/cmd/browse.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/left.rs` (`local_section`), `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/grid.rs` (`folder_header`) | Local: browse Pictures / Desktop / Downloads / Home or any folder without adding it (breadcrumb, Include subfolders, Add N to My Photos); browsed photos stay out of All Photos, albums and counts (album commands skip them and the album menus say to Add to My Photos first, issue #684); folders picked with Browse Folder… (or Keep in Local / Add to Local on a folder row) stay listed across restarts, and browsing a folder inside a listed one highlights it inside that tree (opened down to it, siblings reachable) instead of adding a root; edits go to XMP sidecars; importing promotes them; right-click a top-level location → Remove from Local hides the shortcut only (nothing on disk or in the catalog changes), saved with the UI state, and “Show N hidden locations” (or Browse Folder… on it) brings it back, each on its own from the row's list when several are hidden, or all at once with Restore All (issue #2114); a folder is one location however its path is spelled (`/` or `\`, trailing separator, `.`/`..`, drive-letter case; `folder_key` in `crates/catalog/src/query.rs`); browsed photos nobody changed are forgotten once their folder has not been browsed for 30 days (Settings → Performance → Local folders, 0 = never; checked when the library opens, or `cmd:library.forgetLocal` with `dryRun`; files and sidecars stay, browsing again brings them back; see `crates/catalog/src/local.rs` for what counts as a change); browsing a folder again forgets at once the untouched records of its files that another app deleted or moved, so they no longer fail to load on every visit (issue #1392; changed ones stay, shown as missing); browsing a folder keeps the active photo when the folder shows it, else makes the folder's first photo active as it arrives, the same rule as albums and the other sources (`Session::keep_active_in_view`; issue #1447: before, a library photo stayed active, so Photo Grid marked nothing and Detail, the histogram and the info showed a photo the folder doesn't hold) |
| LR-IMP-SIDECAR-SPLIT | Separate XMP sidecar variants | P2 | ⬜ | `cmd:library.xmpPreferences` | sidecar naming option exists (stem/full), no split sidecars |
| LR-IMP-FORMATS | Supported formats | P0 | 🟡 | `crates/codecs/src/lib.rs`, `crates/raw/src/lib.rs`, `crates/raw/src/jxl.rs` | JPEG, PNG, TIFF (1- to 64-bit integer, 16- to 64-bit float, incl. bilevel and CCITT fax), WebP, JXL, PSD, GIF, BMP (lossy wide-gamut JXL decodes to the right colours: jxl-oxide desaturated XYB rendered to P3/Rec. 2020, worked around in `crates/codecs/src/jxl.rs`); DNG (incl. lossy / Smart Preview DNG and DNG 1.7 JPEG XL tiles/strips: lossless ones decode bit-exactly on synthetic CFA and LinearRaw files, lossy ones are restored to the declared linear primaries without clipping or gamut mapping, checked on one real file with 108 lossy tiles, and any tile that fails to decode makes the file preview-only; DNG 1.7 row/column-interleaved rasters (`RowInterleaveFactor` / `ColumnInterleaveFactor`) are undone before the mosaic is read, so a file from Adobe's converter with a 2×2 interleave no longer decodes as four scaled-down copies in the wrong colours; a dimension the factor does not divide decodes with short trailing fields; verified on 4 real ILCE-6400 JPEG XL DNGs rendering bit-identical to their non-interleaved twins, factors above 2 rest on the plain reading of the tag; a single-chunk JPEG XL preview IFD is offered as the embedded preview; a DNG whose samples pile up at one value well below its stated `WhiteLevel`, as a camera app's 12-bit Bayer DNG that states 4095 and clips at 3567, has those samples raised to the white level on decode, so highlight reconstruction sees the clipped areas instead of white balance tinting them magenta, issue #548, `crates/raw/src/saturation.rs`), CR2 (colour-filter layout from the file's `CR2CFAPattern` tag, issue #85), ARW (pre-2017 bodies: white balance and black level from the encrypted `SR2SubIFD`, issue #148, its `WB_RGGBLevels` being the white balance the camera applied, issue #535; downsized Sony lossless YCbCr 4:2:0 / 4:2:2 tiles decoded to linear RGB, verified on private ILCE-7M4 3:2 & 4:3 examples including 16-bit full-resolution export; Sony DSC-R1 .SR2 decodes, its big-endian 16-bit words read in the order that fits the sample range, checked on the CC0 3221 sample; its crop and white balance live in the SR2SubIFD and are not read yet; ARW6 Compressed / Compressed (HQ) of the ILCE-7RM6 (`crates/raw/src/vendor/arw6.rs`, `crates/raw/src/llvc.rs`; 12-bit codes exact against a black-box oracle on 8 CC0 files, FF over the full frame, APS-C in the interior (LibRaw's own edge rows differ); output through our own companding curve, measured from CC0 lossless / ARW6 pairs; geometry checked against the camera JPEG, `docs/arw6-compression.md`; the ILCE-7M5 writes the same codec, unverified)), NEF (uncompressed, lossless and lossy compressed 12/14-bit, `crates/raw/src/vendor/nefc.rs`; strips labelled 34713 without a `0x0096` table are packed LSB-first 12/14-bit rows, tight or padded to 16 bytes, e.g. the Z 6II's "Packed 14 bits", issue #769; Nikon small raw (sRAW) as 4:2:2 luma/chroma, approximate colour; two-field Coolpix NEFs (E5400, E5700 CYGM, E8400, E8800); as-shot white balance of the D2H, D70 and D70s from the ColorBalance block), Sony ARW version 1 (DSLR-A100 and A200…A390: a 12-bit Huffman-coded column stream, `crates/raw/src/vendor/arw1.rs`, one CC0 file per body verified), RAF (uncompressed, lossless/lossy compressed Bayer and X-Trans; `crates/raw/src/vendor/rafc.rs`, full sensor verification on 13 bodies, `docs/raf-compression.md`), RW2 / Leica RWL / Panasonic RAW in every raw format (compressed 4 and 6, the prefix-coded strips of format 8 from the GH6 on, packed 2/5/7, the 16-bit words of the 2005–2007 bodies; black levels, in-camera aspect crops and mapped-out defect pixels; 178 CC0 files from 118 bodies checked, `crates/raw/src/vendor/rw2.rs`), PEF, ORF (uncompressed and, since 2026-10-13, Olympus's compressed coding of every Olympus / OM body since ~2008: `decode_compressed`, E-M1 and E-M10 Mark III corpus files decode and render correctly, other bodies unverified; colour-filter layout from the file's Exif `CFAPattern`; 12.8 bit/px 16-byte blocks of ten 12-bit samples + a zero pad byte, E-300 / E-330 / E-500 verified on CC0 files, E-M5 Mark II / PEN-F high-res files decode smooth but CFA and black unverified), Kodak DCS520C TIFF (a two-component 12-bit lossless JPEG strip, the two components the even and odd columns; the file's `GrayResponseCurve` table linearises the companded samples to 12 bits, black measured per CFA position, white balance from the `KodakIFD`; `RawFormat::KodakDcs`, `crates/raw/src/vendor/kodak.rs`, checked on the one CC0 sample against its IFD0 thumbnail), Hasselblad 3FR with uncompressed raw data (X1D, X1D II, X2D, CFV 100C: a TIFF without a DNG version tag that describes its CFA IFD, levels, crop and white balance with DNG's tags; `RawFormat::CfaTiff`, read by `dng::decode_as`, RGGB when the file states no `CFAPattern`; the lossless-JPEG 3FR and FFF files use scan selection value 8 and stay preview-only; an FFF's colour matrix comes from its embedded 3FR header, and 3FR / FFF uncompressed RGB previews are read), Samsung SRW without compression (NX5, NX10, NX11 and the NX20-generation bodies NX20, NX200, NX210, NX1000 and NX1100 as 12-bit packed samples in either bit order, EX1 and WB2000 as 16-bit words; the compressed codings 32770 / 32772 / 32773 of the NX30, NX300, NX2000, NX3000, NX mini, NX1 and NX500, `crates/raw/src/vendor/srwc.rs`, checked on 7 CC0 files against the camera JPEG, CFA layout taken from the embedded JPEG because these files state wrong ones; layout, camera-JPEG framing, levels and white balance from the maker note, `crates/raw/src/vendor/srw.rs`; median ΔE vs the camera JPEG 2.2–7.6 on 11 of the 12 files from 10 bodies checked, 10.7 (5.7 registered) on the backlit WB2000 sample), Minolta MRW (16-bit words of the DiMAGE 5 / 7 / 7i / 7Hi, 12-bit packing of the A1, A2, A200, Dynax / Maxxum 5D and 7D; layout, framing and as-shot white balance from the `\0PRD` and `\0WBG` blocks, Exif from `\0TTW`; `crates/raw/src/vendor/mrw.rs`, 7 CC0 files from 6 bodies checked against the camera JPEG), Epson ERF (12-bit 16-byte blocks, maker-note black, white balance measured from the preview; `crates/raw/src/vendor/erf.rs`, R-D1 / R-D1s / R-D1x checked), Mamiya MEF (uncompressed 12-bit CFA sub-IFD of the ZD; `crates/raw/src/vendor/mef.rs`), Canon CRW (CIFF heap, Canon block Huffman coding with all three table sets, 10-bit and 12-bit with low bits, `SensorInfo` framing, masked-column black, as-shot white balance from the file or the camera JPEG; `crates/raw/src/vendor/crw.rs`, 15 Bayer bodies checked against the camera JPEG; CMYG G1, Pro70, Pro90 IS, 600, A5, A50 refused, preview). 12-bit NEF black level: maker note `0x003d` is in 14-bit units (D750, D780, D850, D7500, Z 50 checked). Lossy type 2 NEF curves of version `0x44 0x40` (D780, D6, Z 5, Z 6, Z 7, Z 7_2) span a quarter of the sample range, read from the table's own point spacing. Import probes (`probe_info`) of compressed NEFs decode only the first 128 rows, the ones the masked-trailing-column test reads in both the probe and the full decode (the D5100 and D7000 write 8 masked columns at 0 with no maker-note tag for them, compressed and uncompressed alike), instead of the whole Huffman stream: ~3 ms instead of the full decode time per file (386 ms on a 24 MP D750 file), issue #708. CR3 lossless CRX Bayer and version 0x100/0x200 C-RAW decodes to sensor pixels with timed white balance, black/white levels and file crop (the shot's aspect ratio from the maker note's `AspectInfo`); full sensor samples match the reference on M50/R100/R8/RP, incl. RP 1.6× crop mode (`crates/raw/tests/it/cr3_corpus.rs`, `docs/cr3.md`). EOS R5 Mark II C-RAW and RAW decode too (the QP map's first-symbol literal and 23-zero escape, odd-height Bayer planes): 4 CC0 files pinned, every QP map of 33,665 private C-RAW files decodes exactly, and full-frame images match the camera JPEG. Unsupported coding variants retain metadata and use the embedded JPEG when available; HEVC preview decoding remains unsupported. Nikon High Efficiency NEF (HE / HE★, compression 34713 with a JPEG XS-derived wavelet codestream and no `0x0096` table) decodes from sensor data for the verified four-component profile (`crates/raw/src/vendor/nefhe.rs`, `oxideav-jpegxs`; Z50 II HE and HE★ pinned, `docs/nikon-he.md`; issue #193); other HE profiles stay preview-only with the reason "Nikon High Efficiency NEF". Missing: CR3 unverified CRX variants, unverified Nikon HE profiles (Z 8, Z 9, Z 6III, Z f files not yet checked), NEF "lossy after split" (preview only: imported with `preview_only` = the decoder's reason, rendered as a rendered JPEG, flagged by a grid badge and loupe/Edit/Info notices, `previewOnly` in `catalog.query`; Reload clears it once the file decodes, and the notices carry a Reload from Disk button; each flag is stamped with the app version that set it, and a library opened in another version rechecks its preview-only raws in the background, so a raw a newer release decodes is cleared without a manual Reload, issue #662, `library.recheckPreviewOnly`), TIFF-based raws whose raw data sits in a private block (Phase One / Leaf IIQ, Canon EOS-1D / 1Ds and the Kodak DCS Pro / DCS760C TIFFs (compression 65000): IFD0 is only a reduced RGB image next to a much larger declared picture) are recognised from that structure and import as `preview_only` with the reason, instead of opening as a thumbnail-sized ordinary image (`thumbnail_shell` in `crates/raw/src/lib.rs`), Recognised raws the app cannot decode open as `preview_only` with any usable embedded JPEG, also the CMYG Canon CRW bodies and Sigma X3F (containers recognised by magic, preview found by a structural JPEG scan), Kodak KDC (IFD0 without `ImageWidth`), Leaf MOS (scan), and any decode failure of a recognised raw, not only an unsupported variant; ones with no JPEG still fail with "without an embedded preview" (AVIF decodes with `filmcraft-av1` in native builds, `crates/codecs/src/avif.rs`: 8/10/12-bit, grids, nclx / ICC, PQ / HLG, container rotation / mirror / crop, EXIF / XMP through `crates/meta/src/heif.rs`; alpha and image sequences are not read, and the web build refuses AVIF; HEIC decodes only behind the non-default `heif` feature, which the release packages enable: `crates/heif`, heic-rs for the container and HEVC (bit-exact against libde265 on iPhone tiles) plus our own colour conversion, matching libheif within 1 code value on iPhone-style grids (VUI full range, Display P3 ICC, nclx, 10-bit, irot/imir/clap, gain map/depth ignored); 4:2:0/monochrome only, 4:2:2/4:4:4 Canon/Sony HIF refused; same policy as PhotoCraft), palette (indexed-colour) TIFF (refused by the `tiff` crate); a CR2 opens with the camera's in-camera aspect crop from the maker note's `AspectInfo`, as a CR3 does (`crates/raw/src/vendor/mod.rs` `canon_aspect_crop`) |
| LR-IMP-CAMERA-COVERAGE | Camera coverage, verified per model | P0 | 🟡 | `crates/raw/tests/it/corpus.rs`, `xtask/src/main.rs`, `crates/raw/tests/it/coverage_sweep_corpus.rs`, `docs/raw/evidence.tsv`, `docs/raw/coverage-sweep.tsv` | a supported container is not the same as every camera that writes it decoding correctly: ~55 CC0 corpus files plus 6 exact-sensor CR3 cases and 4 pinned EOS R5 Mark II files (plus ~60 NEFs checked by hand and 178 Panasonic / Leica RW2, RWL and RAW files from 118 bodies, checked against each file's own JPEG) against the >1,000 models Lightroom lists; per-model differences still surface (e.g. the CR2 colour-filter layout differed by model until it was read from the file's own tag, issue #85, checked on 35 CR2 bodies; CR2 black levels were read from border columns light already reaches, left of `SensorInfo`'s border, so CR2s rendered dark with crushed shadows (EOS 6D: 2591 instead of 2047), now measured from the masked columns only, checked on 8 corpus bodies; pre-2017 Sony ARWs opened green until their scrambled white balance / black level were read, issue #148, checked on 16 ARWs from 15 Sony bodies; their white balance now comes from the encrypted `SR2SubIFD`'s `WB_RGGBLevels`, the gains the camera applied, which 14 bodies (DSLR-A450 … A700, NEX-3/5/C3, SLT-A33/A35/A55, ILCE-6001, UMC-R10C) had no other source for and which the maker-note estimate read before missed by up to 100 % on preset shots, issue #535, checked against ExifTool on 100 decoded ARWs from 87 Sony bodies; the encrypted SR2 black level is now read from its directory entry (the DSLR-A450/A500/A550/A700 read 354–975 instead of 512), checked on 126 ARWs from 97 Sony bodies, and downsized lossless M/S ARWs use their measured black level 1024 instead of 512, checked on 12 files from 4 bodies, issue #535, and on a fifth, the ILCE-7CM2 (issue #697: 0.5.0 rendered its lossless M file with that 512 pedestal, the darkest half of a dusk scene +11 L* and blue, the lamplight white; its darkest 0.1 % per channel sits at 1017/1020/1024, and with 1024 the render is within +3 L* of the camera JPEG; the body's compressed and uncompressed "M-size" files are Bayer at 4736×3132 with black 512, two public ones in the corpus); pre-2017 Sony bodies shot in the camera's 16:9 mode (ILCE-7SM2, DSLR-A580) now get the 16:9 crop of their camera JPEG instead of the whole 3:2 frame with padding columns, issue #535; Canon PowerShot and EOS M CR2s whose `ColorData` is stored as bytes opened green until the bytes were read as 16-bit words, 25 files from 25 bodies checked). Compressed RAF sensor arrays verified on 13 Fujifilm bodies (`docs/raf-compression.md`). ILCE-7RM6 ARW6 12-bit codes verified on 8 CC0 files (FF and APS-C, both compression modes). Sony ILCE-7CR (61 MP): lossless compressed L and M and compressed ARW2 samples of one scene in the corpus (black level, default crop, as-shot gains, lossless vs compressed mosaic agreement, fitted camera look), and all nine public codings (also uncompressed, small and APS-C crop) decode and fit. Grow the CC0 corpus per model and test each sample decodes with plausible colour. 2026-10-13: one CC0 file each pinned and decoding for the Z 5, Z 50, Z 6II, Z 7II, Z fc, D750, D780, D850, X100V, X100VI, X-E4, X-S10, X-S20, X-T30 II, EOS 5D Mark IV, 6D Mark II, 2000D, K-1 II, K-3 III, K-70, KF, DSLR-A850 and the nine ARW version 1 bodies (278 of 1,446 Adobe models verified, `docs/raw/coverage.tsv`). Later on 2026-10-13: Z 8 and Z 9 lossless NEFs, and 15 pre-X FinePix bodies once their SuperCCD RAFs decoded (`crates/raw/src/vendor/raf_superccd.rs`, `corpus_fujifilm_superccd`): 295 verified. Nikon CropArea and untagged-preview colour-space regression tests; private Z 8 crop verified; the Nikon D4, which records no CropArea, gets its image area and white level measured on 148 NEFs against the camera JPEG (optical-black stripes and magenta clipped highlights gone). Public raw.pixls.us coverage sweep (#1327): 30 more models verified on 40 CC0 files, with SHA-256 and FNV-1a sensor hashes pinned (`crates/raw/tests/it/coverage_sweep_corpus.rs`, `docs/raw/coverage-sweep.tsv`; `docs/raw/coverage.py`: 515 → 545 verified rows). Render/reference luminance correlation is 0.6801 to 0.9998; eleven candidates excluded for identity, provenance or missing/mismatched reference images. Nikon D6 / Z 6 / Z 7 uncompressed and Z 8 HE variants remain preview-only. Own-matrix DNG tone differs from the camera JPEG (#1277); sensor verification is not colour calibration. Canon CR3 `ColorData` white levels below the sensor's saturation point (EOS RP 14558 under a pile-up at 16383, R100 at ISO 640 10439 under 13300–13700) rise to the data's pile-up when the samples run past them, the change kept in the baseline exposure and out of the camera-look fit, issue #2053 (upstream), checked upstream on 35 CC0 CR3s from 26 bodies. |
| LR-IMP-PREVIEW-READ | Preview reads the header, not the raw | P0 | ✅ | `crates/raw/src/preview.rs` (`bounded_preview`, `embedded_preview_from_range`, `embedded_preview_covering`), `crates/engine/src/files.rs` (`preview_window`), `docs/raw-preview-reads.md` | a grid thumbnail and a loupe preview of an unedited raw show the camera's embedded JPEG, which sits near the start of the file (a 24 MP Fuji RAF keeps its 5.6 MB JPEG in a 56 MB file), so the preview loader reads the header and that JPEG instead of the whole file: 56.5 MB → 5.9 MB and ~10 s → 0.96 s measured on one real RAF over a GVFS/SMB Wi-Fi share (byte-identical preview pixels; `<PREFIX>_PROFILE=1` reports the bytes); the RAF pointer is read from its fixed header fields, formats whose preview only the whole file reveals (CR3 boxes, TIFF pointer tags past the prefix, CRW/MRW/X3F scans) keep the whole-file path, and a lying pointer, a short read or a file that changes underneath us falls back to it rather than losing the preview. An unedited raw's **grid thumbnail is that camera JPEG itself**, so no sensor decode is scheduled behind it (`Session::embedded_thumb_is_final`, `grid::skips_the_real_render`): drawing a 512 px thumbnail used to decode the whole raw — 45 s a photo at 1.2 MB/s, 8.3 GB for a 149-raw folder — and the queue it built delayed the render the user was waiting for; a cached render as stand-in, an edited photo and a raw without an embedded JPEG still render. Stand-ins and grid thumbnails decode the smallest embedded JPEG that covers the requested size and is a copy of the largest (aspect ratio within 1 %, same component count), not always the largest, on the whole-file and the plan route alike: an A7R VI's 1616 × 1080 JPEG (0.3 MB) instead of its 9984 × 6656 one (5.6 MB), and mid-sized NEF / RW2 JPEGs CR3 thumbnails take the smallest embedded copy of the same shape that covers the cell (the 1620 px `PRVW`), and the box search stops at `mdat` (OneSlips). |
| LR-IMP-CULL-AT-IMPORT | Culling analysis at import | P2 | 🟡 | `cmd:photo.analyze` | run Assisted Culling on the imported photos (they're selected after an import); not automatic |
| LR-IMP-MOVE | Move on import [Classic] | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:library.import` (`mode` move), `crates/engine/src/import_move.rs`, `crates/engine/src/tests_import_move.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/import.rs` | Transfer ▸ Move with Copy's destination, folders (day / month / one folder / custom template), rename template and example destination; XMP sidecars (both namings) move along; each source is removed only after its destination is written (hard link on the same volume, else copied, synced and compared byte for byte) and its catalog record is saved; failed, duplicate, unchecked files keep their sources, taken names get -1, -2…, never overwritten; files already in the destination/library are added in place; sources that can't be removed (read-only card) are kept and reported (`kept`). Undo removes the photos from the library but leaves the moved files at the destination. No Copy as DNG while moving |
| LR-IMP-DNG-CONVERT | Convert to DNG on import [Classic] | P2 | ✅ | `cmd:library.import` (`dng`), `crates/ui-egui/src/import.rs` | copy imports: Raw files ▸ Copy as DNG (lossless; the card is untouched); the raw copy is removed only once its DNG is verified and on disk (issue #106) |
| LR-IMP-WINDOW | Import tab, Chromaforge "Browse" (cull a card or folder, then import) | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:view.importTab`, `cmd:view.libraryTab`, `cmd:import.start`, `cmd:import.develop`, `cmd:import.configure`, `cmd:import.targets`, `cmd:import.run`, `cmd:import.savePreset`, `crates/ui-egui/src/import_tab.rs`, `crates/engine/src/cmd/import_tab.rs` | OneSlips' Import tab, the default (Settings ▸ Import ▸ Import as a tab; off = the import review), renamed "Browse": Photo Grid / Grid / Detail views, a Show All / Show Not Imported quick filter (`library.browse {show}`; Show All marks files the library has already In library in the grid), and Develop without Import (the Library tab's Develop module on a browsed photo, edits to the XMP sidecar, the catalog untouched); picks / selection / all, preset stack, import presets, copied records follow their files; Classic, Compact and Modern. A deliberate divergence from the reference app's naming (it kept Import) |
| LR-IMP-NODELETE | Browsed photos never deleted | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:photo.delete`, `cmd:photo.deleteFromDisk`, `cmd:photo.rename`, `crates/engine/src/tests_import_tab.rs` | delete, delete from disk, rename and Delete Rejected leave browsed (not imported) photos and their files alone |

| LR-LIB-PEOPLE | People / faces | P2 | 🟡 | `cmd:faces.detect`, `cmd:view.people`, `cmd:view.faceBoxes`, `cmd:photo.removeRegion`, `cmd:photo.setRegion`, `cmd:photo.addRegion`, `cmd:library.filter`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/people.rs`, `crates/catalog/src/query.rs` (`people`), `crates/engine/src/media.rs` (`face_job`), `crates/meta/src/xmp.rs` | MVP: named face regions read from XMP (MWG-RS, read-only, see `docs/xmp-interop.md`; clipped to the photo; Lightroom's `Rotation` of ±π/2 and π handled, mirrored orientations taken as written; boxes follow Rotate Left/Right and flips; a sidecar stating `mwg-rs:Regions`, even empty, replaces the photo's on re-read, one without leaves them) become cards in the People view (View ▸ People, or the People button in the toolbar): a close-up of the person's largest face, name and photo count; a click filters the grid (`person` in `library.filter`, `person:` search token, filter chip). Face boxes in the loupe can be switched off (View ▸ Loupe Info ▸ Face Boxes, toolbar) resized with eight drag handles and removed with the × shown on hover (catalog-only and undoable, one undo step per drag; the sidecar is never rewritten for either); no moving a box by dragging it yet. Library ▸ Detect Faces (`cmd:faces.detect`) finds faces with the YuNet detector (pure Rust, about 200 ms a photo, matches OpenCV's own YuNet on a 45-photo test set; the 232 KB model is a one-time download offered in the same step, never part of the app) and adds them as unnamed boxes; names are suggested from the faces already named (recognition models are opt-in downloads, run on the CPU in a background thread; a face is named only when you confirm it); a person's page shows their cropped faces and the unnamed faces that look like them (LRC-LIB-PEOPLE), with a user-triggered ↑ action or targeted A key to name all shown suggestions at or above one score in one undo step; with recognition on, a background scan finds faces in photos that have none and embeds every face (one to three worker threads, raws through their embedded preview); an Unnamed faces section orders faces by likeness and names a selected group at once; Photo ▸ People ▸ Draw Face Regions (`cmd:photo.addRegion`) draws a face box by dragging in the loupe and opens its name box; no separated clusters, no writing regions back; Detect Faces runs YuNet at the photo's own shape up to 1024 px (WIDER FACE val hard AP 76.6, was 59.8 at 640 x 640) |

| LR-LIB-DELETE | Delete / Recently Deleted | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:photo.delete`, `cmd:photo.restore`, `cmd:photo.deletePermanently`, `cmd:photo.deleteFromDisk` | Delete and Delete Permanently retain surviving selected photos; if none remain, select the next visible survivor (previous at the end) in the current source/filter/sort; permanent deletion removes catalog records, not files on disk; Restore and Delete Permanently in the Photo menu and the photo context menus for photos in Recently Deleted (before, the UI had no way to restore); importing a file that is in Recently Deleted again restores the photo with its edits, rating and albums (issues #298, #706: before, the import skipped it as "already in the library" while the grid showed it deleted) — the review dialog checks it, badges it "Will be restored", counts it as "in Recently Deleted" rather than "already in the library", and its choice Restore them / Import as new / Leave them starts on Restore; dropped files restore too ("Restored 1 photo from Recently Deleted"); a deleted photo matched by content whose file is gone is relinked to the imported file; Photo ▸ Empty Recently Deleted (`cmd:library.emptyRecentlyDeleted`, one undo step, shown while the trash is the source); import takes `onDeleted` restore (default) / skip / fresh for files in the trash (`cmd:library.import`; the report lists `restored`, and with skip flags the duplicate `existingDeleted`); browsing a folder in Local (`cmd:library.browse`), Auto Import and a Lightroom catalog import never restore a deleted photo; Delete asks first by default from the key, the menu bar and the photo context menu (Settings ▸ General ▸ "Confirm before moving photos to Recently Deleted" turns it off; issue #1239); Delete from Disk (`cmd:photo.deleteFromDisk`, Photo ▸ File ▸ Delete from Disk, and a button in the Delete dialog beside Remove) moves the originals and their XMP sidecars to the system trash (Trash / Recycle Bin / freedesktop trash; never a permanent delete) and takes the photos out of the library in one undo step; virtual copies go with their master, a copy deleted alone keeps the file, a stem sidecar another file shares stays, a missing file (disk not connected) keeps its photo; it always asks first, even with the Confirm setting off, and CLI / MCP / control callers must pass `confirm: true`; the trashing shows in the activity stack and can be cancelled between files (fork #98); not in the web build; Empty Recently Deleted is also a button in the trash view's header (`button:emptyRecentlyDeleted`) and the Recently Deleted row's right-click menu (`menu:emptyRecentlyDeleted`); it and Delete Permanently ask first ("Remove … from the library for good?", files on disk kept) under the same Confirm setting; no auto-purge after N days (needs a deleted-at date in the catalog); Photo ▸ Delete Rejected Photos (`cmd:library.deleteRejected`, ported from OneSlips) takes every rejected photo of the view in one undo step to Recently Deleted, or with `files: recycle` + `confirm: true` through Delete from Disk; the menu item always asks how (remove, or remove and trash the files) |

| LR-LIB-RENAME | Batch rename | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:photo.rename`, `cmd:photo.renamePreview`, `cmd:photo.renameTokens`, `cmd:dialog.rename`, `crates/engine/src/rename.rs` (`TOKENS`) | template tokens {name} {num} {seq:N} {date:%Y%m%d} {folder} {camera} {lens} {iso} {rating} {title} {creator} {ext} (shared with export naming and import rename); a Tags picker beside every template field (Rename Photos, import Rename, export File name) lists each tag with meaning + live example, the date directives and the rules, and inserts the clicked tag at the text cursor; unknown tags are flagged; preview; renames files on disk with their XMP sidecars (a stem sidecar another file still shares, e.g. raw + JPEG, is copied rather than taken), never overwriting (-1, -2… suffixes; a change of letter case only is checked by file identity, so on case-sensitive volumes `img_1.jpg` next to `IMG_1.JPG` is a collision), rolls back on failure (a file that can't be moved back is reported old → new and the library follows it as an undoable partial rename; the same for undo/redo); undo/redo move the files; virtual copies follow; naming presets (`cmd:naming.presets`, `cmd:naming.savePreset`, `cmd:naming.deletePreset`) and `{text:Label}` tags typed at each rename or export (ported from OneSlips) |
| LR-LIB-CAPTURETIME | Edit capture time | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:photo.setCaptureTime`, `cmd:dialog.captureTime`, `crates/catalog/src/dates.rs` | set (the other selected photos shift by the same amount, or `each`), shift by days/hours/minutes, time-zone shift, change to the original file's modification date (`fromFile`, UTC plus an optional zone shift; dialog mode “File date”); read from the file name (`fromFilename`: `IMG_20260921_143000`, `2026-09-21 14.30.00`, `PXL_…`, `IMG-20260921-WA0001` as midnight; names without a date are skipped and counted as `noDateInName`; dialog mode “File name”, issue #619); `dryRun` previews; one undo step, journaled; Metadata panel button; no “revert to original capture time”. Impossible calendar dates cannot anchor relative shifts; rejection preserves metadata, selection, Undo and Redo. A capture time that doesn't fully read (cut short, or text after it) is left as it is and counted (`unreadable`); it can't anchor a set that moves other photos, and set alone repairs it. A year or a month alone (`1983`, `1983-04`) is kept as stated (for one photo, or `each`; the dialog gives it to every selected photo and starts from it; shifts skip it as `noTime`), shown as "1983" / "April 1983", sorted and grouped as the start of its period, and written to XMP `photoshop:DateCreated` only. |

Eyes (ported from OneSlips): the eye focus strip (View ▸ Eye Focus Strip) shows each person's sharper eye at 100 % judged sharp / soft / blurry, closed and half-closed eyes and sunglasses marked (MediaPipe Face Landmarker, downloaded on request), kept in the preview cache; `photo.rejectEyes` rejects closed / blurry eyes, `eyes.analyze` prepares a folder. No expression detection; the dialog has no eyes option yet; the Assisted Culling dialog asks for closed / blurry (or soft) eyes and main subjects only; suggested 1–5 stars and a Pick / Reject flag per photo from the combined score (issue #489), applied by the dialog only to photos the user has not rated or flagged (`applyRatings` / `applyFlags`), stored as `Analysis.suggested` and filterable as the smart-collection rule field `suggestedStars` |

## C. Views & navigation (VIEW)

| Id | Feature | Tier | Status | Evidence | Missing / notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LR-VIEW-PHOTOGRID | Justified photo grid | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:view.photoGrid`, `cmd:library.groups`, `cmd:library.sort` (`group`), `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/grid.rs`, `crates/catalog/src/dates.rs` | grouped by capture / import / edit date (day headers “Wednesday, 30 September 2026 · 12 photos”, months or years when zoomed out, or a fixed day/month/year/none choice in the sort menu); sticky header; clicking a header selects its photos; stacks never split; scrolls to the active photo only when it changes (or the grid comes back on screen), never on its own; a mouse-wheel notch scrolls a third of the grid's height, in both grids, as in Lightroom Classic (`theme::WHEEL_STRIP_SHARE`, scaled by the Wheel scroll speed preference; trackpads scroll the points they report); a double-click opens the photo in the loupe, or in Develop with Preferences → Interface → Grid → Double-click opens the photo in Develop (`doubleClickDevelop`, off by default as in Lightroom Classic; fork #144) |
| LR-VIEW-SQUAREGRID | Square grid | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:view.squareGrid` | same date headers as the photo grid |
| LR-VIEW-DETAIL | Single-photo view | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:view.detail`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/detail.rs` | a “Rendering…” pill at the bottom of the photo (widget `loupe:rendering`; above the Navigator when that is in the way) once a render has been on its way for 0.5 s with nothing new on screen: over a stand-in (the raw's embedded JPEG, a thumbnail), the previous render after an edit, or, zoomed in, the magnified frame waiting for its window; Before views watch their own renders. Always on (Lightroom Classic makes it a View Options choice). When the render fails behind a stand-in, a caution pill says “This photo can't be opened” (“Original offline · no smart preview” when that is why) with the reason on hover and for screen readers (widget `loupe:renderFailed`; `ui.inspect` → `loupe.failed`) |
| LR-VIEW-EDIT | Edit view | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:panel.edit` | |
| LR-VIEW-FULLSCREEN | Full-screen preview | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:view.fullScreenPreview`, `cmd:view.enterFullScreen` | photo on black, arrows step, Esc exits; ⇧⌘F window full screen |
| LR-VIEW-FILMSTRIP | Filmstrip | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:view.filmstrip`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/detail.rs` | the photo context menu on right-click; the mouse wheel scrolls it sideways, a third of its width per notch as in Lightroom Classic (trackpads scroll the points they report; Preferences → Interface → Wheel scroll speed scales it); it follows the active photo only when that changes (centred if off screen), so a scrolled strip stays put; every selected photo is highlighted, the active one framed in white, as in the grid; a source line above the thumbnails shows the source, the photo count, the number selected and the active photo's file name (a shade brighter; source, then name, then counts are cut short when narrow), widgets `film:count` and `film:fileName`; prepared adjacent views are adopted using the loupe job key; arbitrary jumps can still wait for a render (PR #506) |
| LR-VIEW-ZOOM | Zoom & pan | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:view.zoomFit`, `cmd:view.zoom100`, `cmd:view.zoomIn`, `cmd:view.zoomOut`, `cmd:view.zoomToggle`, `cmd:view.clickZoom`, `cmd:view.navigate`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/detail.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/headless.rs` | continuous native pinch / modifier-wheel zoom (Fit–1100%, anchored under the pointer), two-finger scroll / touch pan with edge limits; Detail (including tools, Before/After and full-screen preview), Compare and Reference; fractional percentages preserve small gestures; image, picker and targeted-adjustment cursors stay steady between gesture events; Zoom In / Out step through the levels 1:4, 1:3, 1:2, 1:1, 2:1, 3:1, 4:1, 8:1, 11:1 and `view.zoomLevel` names any of them or Fit / Fill (`state::ZOOM_LEVELS`); Fill in the bottom bar; click / Z / Space eases to the chosen ratio (1:1 default, 2:1, 3:1, 4:1, 8:1, 11:1). 1:1 tiles equal the full-size export pixel for pixel, across tile seams too (within 2 levels, `tests_previews::one_to_one_pixels_equal_full_resolution_export_pixels`; all 87 corpus raws pass `one_to_one_pixels_equal_export_pixels_on_corpus_raws`); panning at 1:1 on 45 MP keeps up with ≈ 308 fps (CPU) / ≈ 115 fps (GPU) of 40 px frames with one render worker (plan/STATUS.md → Metrics). Zoomed views are a **tile grid** (`region::tiles_for`, `region::TileCache`, `Slot::Tile`): 1024 px tiles of the zoomed frame, each rendered with its margin of context and cropped back, cached by photo, side, frame size and look (settings, overlay, proof, draft) in an LRU bounded to 384 MB of textures (96 MB in the browser), so a pan renders only the tiles it moves onto (a ring of one tile around the view is prepared ahead) and undoing an edit finds its tiles still there; tiles scrolled away before their turn are dropped from the queue. A built 1:1 preview (Library → Previews) stands in for the tiles on screen in the first frame, before they render (`Session::full_preview_in_memory`). Mask / spot / Point Color overlays and soft proofing are tiled too (each tile carries the overlay or proof). The loupe's cost follows the canvas, not the zoom (issue #323): the whole frame is rendered at about canvas size (at most the 2560 px preview source level unless a larger Preview size is chosen, and never above the GPU's texture side), and once the view is zoomed well past that (more than 25 %) the window on screen is rendered too, cut from a frame of at most the photo's own pixels, so beyond 100 % the GPU magnifies it (`region::plan`, `RenderRequest::window`, `Session::region_job`, `crates/ui-egui/src/region.rs`); 1:1 and 2:1–8:1 are sharp at any photo size. A slider drag at a zoomed view drafts the window on screen as one job per pane with its own stage cache (`Slot::Window`), drawn over the tiles until the final look's tiles arrive: a tick takes 6–10 ms on a 1400 × 900 canvas and 9–19 ms on a 2800 × 1700 one at 100 % and 400 % of a 24 MP photo (Radeon 8060S, GPU, `profile_slider_drag`; drafting each tile apart took 120–250 ms); about 19 ms at 100 % on the big canvas is over a 16 ms frame. A continuous pinch holds the render sizes (and the windows there are) until it is quiet. A fit view on a canvas up to about 3200 px (a Retina Mac) never decodes the original; larger displays get a window at fit, which does. Photos whose spots or Auto Mask strokes read too far for one tile render (only photos longer than 8192 px) render the whole frame at the size it is drawn, as before windows; Compare and Reference views get their own tiles per photo (pane) the same way; while a preset or profile is hovered, or over a picture that is not the frame (an unsupported raw's embedded JPEG), the whole frame stays canvas-sized. Before/After (side by side, stacked, `\\` and both wipes) gets the same windows on both sides, clipped to their pane or side of the line. Tiles render on the GPU like whole frames (equivalence-tested against the CPU), in the browser build too (tiles no wider than its GPU's texture side), and are not made while hovering a look, nor over a picture that is not the frame (an unsupported raw's embedded JPEG with another crop). No texture is larger than the GPU allows. Spots and Auto Mask brushes work while what a window reads fits one render (8192 px along an axis, 4096 in the browser): the window grows to hold the spot and its source patch, or the dabs near it, and if that would take more (a clone from far away at 8:1) the loupe keeps the whole-frame render instead, and spots with an automatic source pick it from a fixed-size render of the whole frame (so a window's choice can differ from a whole-frame render's, which picks at its own resolution). The wide stages (dehaze, clarity, highlights/shadows base) and Refine Edges on masks read a window margin capped at 768 px; measured on hard-edged blocks that costs at most a few levels at 8:1. the histogram and Navigator come from the whole-frame render. Compare and Reference get the same windows, one per pane (`Slot::CompareRegion`, `panels/compare.rs`), over each pane's whole-frame render (at most 2560 px); while either view is open two originals stay decoded (`MediaCache::set_full_capacity`), so one pane's window does not evict the other's source; a pinch, or a slider drag on the active photo in Reference, keeps the windows there are until it ends.  Settings > Performance offers optional high quality adjustment previews: settled processing, render sizes and display fit during a drag, with one native-resolution window per zoomed pane; a drag whose full-quality updates are too slow (median over 100 ms) drafts while the slider moves and renders the settled quality once it holds still for 0.2 s, mouse still down, and that slider on that photo starts its next drags so for a while (`region::HqDrag`, `perf.adjustQuality`); adaptive drafts remain the default. See `docs/high-quality-adjustment-previews.md` and `crates/ui-egui/src/tests_adjustment_quality.rs`.; high quality updates use byte/GPU display fitting; a released zoomed edit uses one full-quality window before its tile ring; idle original preparation is opportunistic and not a guarantee for large RAWs (PR #506) A still photo refines to the full render 0.15 s (plus the last full render's time, at most 1 s) after it stops changing, also while the interaction stays open (`region::StillRefine`); the loupe's drafts start beside a running full render and share its decode (`JobPool::submit_overtaking`, `decode_share`); masks' alpha planes are kept with the view's stages (OneSlips). |
| LR-VIEW-FOCUSPOINT | Camera focus point overlay and zoom (beyond Lightroom) | P2 | 🟡 | `cmd:view.focusPoint`, `cmd:view.zoomToFocus`, `cmd:view.zoomToggle`, `cmd:photo.reload`, `cmd:photo.readFocusPoints`, `cmd:focus.pump`, `crates/meta/src/focus.rs`, `crates/meta/src/focus_read.rs`, `crates/engine/src/focus_scan.rs`, `crates/engine/src/tests_focus_scan.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/detail.rs` (`focus_box`, `draw_focus_point`), `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/compare.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/tests_focus_point.rs`, `docs/focus-points.md`, `cmd:view.spaceZoomsTo` | an extra (Lightroom shows no focus points): the AF frame read from the file at import; photos imported earlier get it in the background from a header-only read of each file (about 32 KB, ~100 photos/s from a spinning disk; activity row, cancellable, resumable; Metadata ▸ Read Focus Points) as a box on the upright photo, drawn in Detail, Compare and Survey through rotation and crop (View ▸ Loupe Info ▸ Show Focus Point, ⌥F); Toggle Zoom centres on it (View ▸ Loupe Info ▸ Zoom to Focus Point, on by default); Info panel row `AF-C · Wide · Animal Eye Tracking`; `meta.focus` in `photo.inspect`. Sony ILCE-1 and ILCE-7SM3 (layout checked against 333 files) and Canon CR3 / CR2 / JPEG from the AFInfo2 / AFInfo3 maker-note record (`crates/meta/src/focus_canon.rs`; checked by OneSlips on EOS R5 C CR3s and 5D Mark IV / 6D CR2s). Partial: other Sony bodies, Nikon, Fujifilm not yet; manual focus and centre-only Wide reports show nothing; Compare panes share one pan, so Z centres on the active photo's point only; Space zooms to the main subject's eyes without a focus point, or eyes first (View ▸ Zoom ▸ Space Zooms To, `view.spaceZoomsTo`) |

| LR-VIEW-BEFOREAFTER | Before / after | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:view.showOriginal`, `cmd:view.beforeAfter`, `cmd:view.beforeAfterSplit`, `cmd:view.beforeAfterTopBottom`, `cmd:view.beforeAfterSplitTopBottom`, `cmd:beforeAfter.setBefore`, `cmd:beforeAfter.copyAfterToBefore`, `cmd:beforeAfter.copyBeforeToAfter`, `cmd:beforeAfter.swap`, `cmd:beforeAfter.resetBefore` | all four layouts; before = the import state (defaults + import preset) or a chosen history step / version / the current settings; copy and swap (View → Before/After Settings, History row menu; LrC shortcuts ⌘⌥⇧← copy After to Before, ⌘⌥⇧→ Before to After, ⌘⌥⇧↑ swap). The chosen before lasts for the session; in side by side and top / bottom, clicks with the white-balance selector, colour samplers and targeted adjustment work in the Before view too (`crates/ui-egui/src/tests_before_after.rs`). | LR-VIEW-COMPARE | Compare two photos | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:view.compare`, `cmd:compare.swap`, `cmd:compare.makeSelect`, `cmd:compare.set`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/compare.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/tests_compare.rs` | select / candidate, synced zoom + pan, arrows move the candidate; a filmstrip click puts the photo into the active side (a photo on the other side swaps), so the active photo is always one of the two shown; a ⌘/Ctrl- or Shift-click on the filmstrip changes the selection as in the grid while the two photos shown stay selected and the active photo is unchanged (#187); 1:1 to 8:1 sharp on both sides (zoom windows, see LR-VIEW-ZOOM); no zoom-link toggle; each pane says “Rendering…” (widget `compare:rendering:<pane>`) or “This photo can't be opened” (`compare:renderFailed:<pane>`) as the loupe does |

| LR-VIEW-INFOOVERLAY | Info overlay on the photo | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:view.infoOverlay`, `cmd:view.viewOptions`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/detail.rs` (`info_overlay`) | off / Info 1 (by default file name, capture date, size) / Info 2 (file name, exposure, camera + lens), each three lines chosen in View Options; ⌘I cycles (I in full screen; elsewhere I stays the Info panel); the star rating and flag show under the lines when the photo has them, and in the full-screen preview on macOS the overlay starts below the window controls (issue #2460) |

| LR-VIEW-HISTOGRAM | Histogram | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:view.histogram`, `cmd:view.colorReadout`, `cmd:view.colorReadoutRgb`, `cmd:view.colorReadoutHsl`, `cmd:view.colorReadoutLab`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/edit.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/color_readout.rs`, `crates/color/src/lab.rs`, `crates/pipeline/src/lib.rs` (`hdr_histogram`) | the colour under the pointer in the loupe shows under the histogram (in place of the EXIF line) as R / G / B %, HSL or CIE L*a*b* D50, picked in the histogram's context menu or View ▸ Loupe Info (persisted); the values are those of the sRGB image the histogram counts (a display-profile render converted back to sRGB), not Lightroom's wide-gamut working RGB, so RGB % can differ from Lightroom's on saturated colours while Lab matches; colour pickers and Point Color swatches show all three on hover; HDR edits: SDR on the left half, the HDR range (0 to 5 stops above SDR white, a fixed scale) on the right with the headroom limit marked; colour bar (white, or Visualize HDR range's colours when that is on) and SDR / HDR labels; the plot itself carries the tonal zones of LRC-DEV-HISTOGRAM-DRAG (hover for a readout, drag to adjust, double-click to reset), and is drawn in the mode `view.histogram.mode` selected and at the scale `view.histogram.scale` selected (LR-VIEW-HISTOGRAM-MODES); it counts only pixels that show the photo: the blank border past its edge that lens corrections or perspective leave stays on the canvas but out of the histogram, the scopes and the clipping indicators, on the CPU and GPU, with a display profile and for HDR edits (issue #211; `lcc_raster::Coverage`, `Frame::coverage`). GPU renders bin it (and the scopes) on the GPU, `crates/gpu/src/wgsl/hist.wgsl`, exactly as the CPU does. A thumbnail served from the preview cache still counts it until the loupe renders |
| LR-VIEW-HISTOGRAM-MODES | Multiple histogram & scope views [issue #206] | P2 | ✅ | `cmd:view.histogram.mode`, `cmd:view.histogram.scale`, `crates/raster/src/histogram.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/histogram.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/edit.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/tests_histogram_modes.rs` | five modes, selected by the panel's own selector (bottom-left of the plot), its right-click menu (above the colour-readout choice), a shortcut the user binds, the control channel or MCP: **RGB Overlay** (the default) draws the three channels additively — their pairwise overlaps read yellow / cyan / magenta and all three read neutral light grey — with the perceptual luminance as a hairline over them; **Luminance** draws that luminance alone; **Separated Channels** splits the plot into three stacked tracks on one shared scale, so a channel's own clipping and colour-balance shifts are unambiguous; **Chroma** draws HSV saturation, `(max − min) / max` of the display-encoded pixels, as one curve; **Vectorscope** draws the 2D chrominance distribution (`Cb` right, `Cr` up, 65 × 65 grid with the neutral origin at the centre) inside a circular graticule with 25 % / 50 % / 75 % / 100 % saturation rings, the 123° skin-tone (“I”) line and the six 75 %-saturation primary / secondary target boxes (`R`, `Mg`, `B`, `Cy`, `G`, `Yl`), each populated cell coloured by its own `(Cb, Cr)` hue and saturation. Two scales: **Linear** against the 99th percentile of the bins (98th of the populated scope cells, so a single spike — a crushed black, a blown sky, a neutral background — never flattens the rest) and **Logarithmic**, `log10(1 + bin)` against the same peak, which lifts the quiet end. Both values are saved with the UI state and read leniently, so a file another build wrote opens on the defaults. The clipping indicators are triangles filled with the colour of the channels that clip (white for all three, yellow / cyan / magenta for a pair), and resting the pointer on one previews the `Overlay::Clipping` mask. **Waveform** draws the photo's luminance column by column, left to right as in the photo with black at the bottom, over a 0 / 25 / 50 / 75 / 100 % graticule, and **RGB Parade** its red, green and blue the same way in three tracks side by side (128 columns × 64 levels per plane, filled with the histogram; narrow tracks merge columns; HDR edits read display-encoded levels, SDR white and above at the top; only the loupe's histogram keeps the grids) |
| LR-VIEW-HDR-DISPLAY | HDR display output | P2 | 🟡 | `apps/app/src/hdr_display.rs` (`levels`, `WindowHdr`), `crates/engine/src/hdr_texture.rs` (`encode_pixel`), `apps/app/src/hdr_probe.rs`, `vendor/egui-wgpu` (`hdr_surface_format`), `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/detail.rs` (`hdr_view_job`) | An HDR edit shows above SDR white on an HDR display (macOS EDR, Windows HDR via scRGB, Wayland: headroom from the compositor's colour management, HDR metadata via `crates/hdrmeta`): `Rgba16Float` extended-linear surface where offered, UI at paper white (`sdr_white_nits / 80` on Windows), highlights up to the display's headroom (re-read every frame); SDR displays unchanged; `<PREFIX>_HDR_DISPLAY=0` opts out; where the compositor offers no extended-linear colour space (GNOME / Mutter) an `Rgba16Float` BT.2100 PQ surface instead (SDR white at the compositor's reference white or 203 cd/m²); `--hdr-probe` diagnostic; checked on Windows 11 / RTX 4090 (DX12 scRGB, headroom ≈ 12.9×) Linux KWin 6.7.5 EDR on a laptop panel (headroom 1.4× read from the compositor) and macOS EDR on an Apple M1 Max XDR display. Zoomed (1:1) views draw an HDR render of the part on screen over their tiles; Before/After (the Before when its settings are HDR), Compare / Reference and the second window show HDR edits in HDR too (`crates/ui-egui/src/panels/detail.rs` `request_hdr`, `hdr_window`; one texture per view, a few per zoomed pane); View ▸ Loupe Info ▸ HDR Preview (`cmd:view.hdrPreview`, MENU-VIEW-HDR) shows the SDR rendition instead. Platform limit: the X11 / XWayland window (Linux default unless an output reports HDR headroom) is SDR, since X11 has no HDR surface or headroom protocol. Slider drags show live HDR drafts of the edited photo's views, zoomed in too, newest first (`crates/ui-egui/src/render.rs` `hdr_frame`); each zoomed pane keeps its last few HDR windows (`reuse_hdr`), encoded on the render worker, where the GPU has room (`hdr_cache_fit`, `crates/gpumem`: at most a quarter of its memory; Settings ▸ Display & Speed greys the choice out and says why elsewhere), and frees their textures once they go; HDR view texels can be encoded on the GPU at the output headroom, with CPU fallback and a remaining readback/upload round trip (PR #506) |

| Id | Feature | Tier | Status | Evidence | Missing / notes |

| LR-META-INFO | Info panel | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:panel.info`, `cmd:photo.setMeta`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/right.rs` (`info`, `camera_card`) | camera card (camera, lens, size, format, focal length / shutter / aperture / ISO); title, caption, alt text, extended description, copyright, copyright status, rights usage terms, copyright info URL, creator; file name (rename), file path (reveal), capture time (edit); location, city, state, country; GPS. No flash, map snippet or people; a raw with no lens name (Nikon DSLRs) shows its range from Exif LensSpecification or the Nikon maker note's Lens tag, written like Lightroom's `24.0-70.0 mm f/2.8` |
| LR-META-COPYRIGHT-DEFAULT | Default copyright on import | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:library.preferences` (`import.copyright`, `import.creator`), `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/settings.rs` | Settings → Import → Metadata; fills only photos without their own |
| LR-META-LOCATION | Location editing | P2 | 🟡 | `cmd:photo.setMeta` (`location`, `city`, `state`, `country`, `countryCode`, `gps`), `cmd:metadata.reverseGeocode` | place fields, ISO country code and GPS (decimal or degrees / minutes / seconds) edited in Info, read/written as IPTC Core / Photoshop / EXIF XMP (`Iptc4xmpCore:CountryCode`); Show on Map opens OpenStreetMap; Fill Location fills empty City / State / Country from GPS offline (GeoNames, downloaded on request: `cmd:metadata.downloadPlaceNames`); no embedded map; a placeholder EXIF GPS block (NUL refs, 0/0 rationals) and an out-of-range XMP position read as no location |
| LR-META-COPYPASTE | Copy / paste metadata | P2 | ✅ | `cmd:photo.copyMetadata`, `cmd:photo.pasteMetadata` | title, caption, alt text, extended description, copyright (notice, status, usage terms, info URL), creator, place fields, keywords; `fields` picks a subset |
| LR-META-XMP | XMP read/write | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:photo.saveMetadataToFile`, `cmd:photo.readMetadataFromFile`, `cmd:library.xmpPreferences`, `cmd:folder.saveMetadata`, `cmd:folder.readMetadata`, `crates/engine/src/sidecar.rs`, `docs/xmp-interop.md` | Read Metadata from File also reads the XMP embedded in a JPEG that has no sidecar (first complete standard APP1 before the image data; no extended XMP), and a sidecar that is found but unreadable fails rather than falling back; sidecar wins for metadata, GPS included (a sidecar's `exif:GPSLatitude`/`GPSLongitude` replaces the camera's embedded position, and a save keeps it as written unless it was changed here; a position cleared here is removed from the sidecar while it still states that one, a different one written by another app since stays); a sidecar capture time (`exif:DateTimeOriginal` / `photoshop:DateCreated` / `xmp:CreateDate`) fills in only when the file has none; saving merges into an existing sidecar (`crates/meta/src/xmp_merge.rs`): another app's `crs:`, `xmpMM:History` and unknown namespaces are kept byte for byte (ownership follows namespace URI: foreign namespaces using `lc:` or `xmp:` cannot supply native settings or ratings and survive saving), an unreadable sidecar is backed up first; with stem naming, files sharing a stem (raw + JPEG) get separate sidecars (`<file>.xmp` for all but the raw; an import ranks the files it is adding the same way, so a TIFF or JPEG arriving beside its raw reads its own `<file>.xmp` and never the raw's `IMG_0001.xmp`, issue #1905); a `crs:` custom white balance on a relative-WB raw is shifted from `crs:AsShotTemperature` rather than read as Kelvin, or kept As Shot and reported when the packet has no as-shot white (issue #510); heal and clone spots (`crs:RetouchAreas`, legacy `crs:RetouchInfo`) are read into our spots (`crates/engine/src/crs_masks.rs`, see `docs/xmp-interop.md` → Spot removal); radial masks (`Mask/CircularGradient`) keep Lightroom's inside / outside: `crs:Flipped="true"` is the effect-inside flag of current masks and of the legacy Radial Filter, `crs:MaskInverted` the inversion, checked against Lightroom Classic's renders (issue #1815); Read Metadata from File never writes the sidecar back, even with auto-write on, so another app's later `crs:` edits are still read (issue #934); `folder.saveMetadata` / `folder.readMetadata {path, background?}` save or read every library photo of a folder and the folders inside it, with a row in the activity stack (upstream #2941); a sidecar with crop edges or an angle but no `HasCrop` imports its crop, as a catalog does (presets still need `HasCrop`) |
| LR-META-EXIF-FULL | Full EXIF/IPTC [Classic] | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:photo.allMetadata`, `cmd:dialog.allMetadata`, `crates/meta/src/tags.rs`, `crates/meta/src/exif.rs`, `crates/meta/src/iptc.rs`, `crates/meta/src/heif.rs` | read and written on export; Info ▸ All Metadata…: every TIFF / EXIF / GPS / interop tag (named, common values spelled out) and the XMP fields, searchable; PSD/PSB EXIF resource 1058 and XMP resource 1060 feed the same metadata rows and preserve sidecar precedence; metadata presets; the exposure time is kept at its own step when read and written back (`lcc_meta::exposure_display_of`: 0.4 s used to round to `1/2` and export as 0.5 s, issue #1619); HEIC, HEIF and AVIF: the primary image's `Exif` and XMP items (or those of the images it is made from, such as a grid's or an `iden` turn's input) are read from the container alone, in builds with or without the HEIC decoder (issue #693); Fujifilm film-simulation settings (film recipe) read from the MakerNote and shown in the Metadata panel with Copy recipe (`cmd:photo.fujiRecipe`, `crates/meta/src/fuji.rs`, upstream #1209) |

## F. Edit panel — global adjustments (EDIT)

| Id | Feature | Tier | Status | Evidence | Missing / notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LR-EDIT-AUTO | Auto settings | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:develop.auto`, `crates/pipeline/src/auto.rs` | a Lightroom-like recipe calibrated black-box on Lightroom's Auto results for 24 Sony raws: Exposure from the scene's scene-linear log-average luminance (Lightroom's observed slope, -0.41 EV per EV), Highlights -66 and Shadows +52 (Lightroom's average Auto values), Contrast +68 and Vibrance +30 fitted on our scales. Held out one photo at a time: on the 16 photos whose start Sony's DRO doesn't brighten, a median dE76 of 6.4 from Lightroom's Auto results against 8.3 unedited; on all 24, 7.7 against 7.9 (issues #318, #209). Calibrated on Sony files only. Per photo (2026-10-13): Highlights / Shadows scale with the frame's bright / dark share, Contrast with its 5–95 % luminance spread, Whites / Blacks bring the 99.5 / 0.5 % luminance just short of white / black; centred so the median of 12 corpus raws (several makes) gets the calibrated values (Contrast 68, Highlights about -60, Shadows about 55, Whites/Blacks about 0). The Lightroom reference set isn't in the repo, so the per-photo terms aren't yet validated against Lightroom's Auto results. The Auto button greys out while the tone sliders still hold what Auto set, until any of them changes (session state, not saved with the edit) |
