| LR-EDIT-LIGHT-HIGHLIGHTS | Highlights | P0 | ✅ | `ctl:light.highlights`, `crates/raw/src/highlight.rs` | sensor-clipped highlights stay neutral when Highlights (or Exposure, Whites) darkens them: fully clipped content is rebuilt to the neutral of the colour model applied after it, partly clipped content takes its colour from reliable unclipped content nearby (not from dark objects, lens fringes or another surface beside it) and white where there is none, and pale partly clipped content (a white sky) turns white on its way to clipping (#523); binned previews and thumbnails decide clipping per sample before averaging (`develop_binned_masked`, `highlight::reconstruct_masked`), so a block with one clipped specular sample keeps its mean instead of jumping to the clip level, which reconstruction turned into green speckles at 500 px (#548) From process V2 the Highlights/Shadows base is the edge-aware filter of the 0.15 %-of-long-edge median of log luminance: twigs and fine texture take their surroundings' shift, so bright areas keep their local contrast as with Exposure (V1's base kept ~0.6 of it at −100, #632); region edges stay sharp |
| LR-EDIT-LIGHT-SHADOWS | Shadows | P0 | ✅ | `ctl:light.shadows` | From process V2, the same base as Highlights: dark areas keep their local contrast when lifted (#632) |
| LR-EDIT-LIGHT-WHITES | Whites | P0 | ✅ | `ctl:light.whites` | |
| LR-EDIT-LIGHT-BLACKS | Blacks | P0 | ✅ | `ctl:light.blacks` | |
| LR-EDIT-LIGHT-CURVE-PARAM | Parametric curve | P0 | ✅ | `ctl:curve.highlights`, `ctl:curve.lights`, `ctl:curve.darks`, `ctl:curve.shadows`, `ctl:curve.split*` | the graph draws the master Point Curve stacked on the region curve, as rendered (a Point Curve preset shows in the line); dragging the line moves the region slider under the pointer |
| LR-EDIT-LIGHT-CURVE-POINT | Point curve | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:develop.curve`, `cmd:curve.reset`, `cmd:curve.presets`, `cmd:curve.applyPreset`, `cmd:curve.savePreset`, `cmd:curve.deletePreset`, `cmd:curve.importPresets`, `cmd:curve.exportPresets`, `cmd:file.importCurvePresets`, `cmd:file.exportCurvePresets`, `crates/engine/src/cmd/curves.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/edit.rs` | click to add a point on the curve (the photo doesn't change until it is dragged, issue #340); Input / Output fields (0–255) place the selected point exactly; drag a point in both axes (between its neighbours, input / output readout, one undo step per drag; ⌥-drag moves it a tenth as far for fine adjustment, issue #1363); drag empty space to add and drag; double-click removes; reset: double-click a channel selector (that channel), the Reset button under the graph (every curve incl. parametric) or right-click the graph (channel / all); Point Curve preset dropdown: own Linear / Medium Contrast / Strong Contrast, user presets (save the current point curves, delete by right-click; saved with the library), import / export as `.lccurve` JSON |
| LR-EDIT-LIGHT-CURVE-RGB | Per-channel curves | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:develop.curve` (`channel`) | |

| LR-EDIT-COLOR-TEMP | Temperature | P0 | ✅ | `ctl:wb.temp`, `crates/pipeline/src/local.rs` (`wb_matrix_for`, `legacy_white`), `crates/raw/src/color.rs` (`rebalance`), `crates/raw/src/spectral.rs` (`find_camera`), `crates/camdb/src/lib.rs` (`find_camera`, `has_matrices_camera`), `crates/catalog/src/model.rs` (`relative_wb_camera`), `crates/develop/src/controls.rs` (`ControlSpec::position`), `crates/raw/src/kelvin_presets.rs` | Kelvin (2000–50000 K, As Shot read from the file) on raws with colour matrices: in the file (DNG: re-developed for the chosen white through the camera's own matrices, camera-space white balance as Lightroom; the eyedropper / Auto invert the same model) or measured for the camera model (`lcc_raw::spectral`, 52 bodies, issue #730: the matrices turn the as-shot multipliers into Kelvin and the slider back, the look stays the file's fit, and the picture is adapted from the as-shot white, Bradford), or read from the camera's own white-balance presets (Sony ARW `SR2SubIFD`, Sony ILCA-99M2, ILCA-77M2, SLT-A33, DSC-RX10, ILCE-3500, ILCE-7S: Sony's Kelvin dial reads back within 0.3 % on 5 settings of 4 bodies; Canon CR2 `ColorData` presets, PowerShot G1 X Mark III: the camera's own As Shot temperature reads back within 4 % on its 9 white-balance settings; Nikon Z5II from a built-in table of its own preset multipliers (`crates/raw/src/kelvin_builtin.rs`): Natural Auto reads back at 5036 K against the camera's 5030 K), or, failing those, through the camera database's matrices (`lcc_camdb`: dcraw's table, about 550 models up to 2018; one D65 matrix, so tungsten readings are less exact); raws without matrices anywhere and rendered files use the relative −100..+100 scale (6500 K / 0 = as shot). The Kelvin slider's track is Lightroom's: 7000 K in the middle, 2000–7000 K evenly on the left half, logarithmic up to 50000 K on the right (within 2 % of positions measured in Lightroom Classic), for drawing, clicks and drags; wheel / arrow-key nudges and Quick Develop still step a fixed 50 / 100 / 500 K; the relative slider stays linear. Settings written on the relative scale before a camera's matrices reached it keep their look (`WbScale`). Not verified against Lightroom's Kelvin: on the ILCE-7M3 the matrices read 7–10 % below Sony's dial (issue #730), and whether the matrices or the dial are off is open; raws without matrices or presets anywhere and rendered files use the relative −100..+100 scale (6500 K / 0 = as shot). Settings written on the relative scale before a camera's matrices reached it keep their look (`WbScale`). Not verified against Lightroom's Kelvin: on the ILCE-7M3 the matrices read 7–10 % below Sony's dial (issue #730), and whether the matrices or the dial are off is open |

| LR-EDIT-OPTICS-PROFILE | Lens profile corrections | P1 | 🟡 | `ctl:optics.profileDistortion`, `ctl:optics.profileVignetting`, `crates/pipeline/src/optics.rs`, `crates/raw/src/sensor_window.rs`, `crates/raw/src/vendor/rw2.rs`, `crates/raw/src/vendor/raf_lens.rs`, `crates/engine/src/crs.rs` | uses corrections embedded in the file: DNG `WarpRectilinear` / `WarpRectilinear2` (opcode 14, DNG 1.6: degree-14 radial polynomial, valid-radius clamp, reciprocal option, applied in list order with the spec's skip rule for a `WarpRectilinear` after an optional one, so Pixel DNGs' two-stage warp is corrected; up to 3 chained stages, sampled on the CPU because the polynomials need `f64`; issue #635) / `FixVignetteRadial`, and the camera's own distortion correction in Panasonic / Leica RW2 / RWL files (tag `0x0119`, read as the equivalent `WarpRectilinear`; on 79 photos from 13 bodies and 11 lenses the corrected render lines up with the camera JPEG within 0.75 px at 1440 px at the corners, from up to 13 % of the half diagonal uncorrected; issue #256); an imported preset's `LensProfileEnable=0` leaves them on, as Lightroom applies built-in corrections regardless (inferred); Sony ILCE-7RM4A Bayer ARW raw-IFD distortion tables (`0x7037`, 16-sample layout, native 3:2 framing) use the same warp path, enabled only for the independently validated ILCE-7RM4A with FE 24–105 and FE 200–600 lenses; ILCE-7RM6 verified against the camera JPEG on 54 photos with three lenses, and the APS-C ZV-E10 (its own unit 16384; 4 photos with the E PZ 16-50 mm, 0.64 px worst) on the same conversion (`docs/sony-ilce7rm6-lens-corrections.md`); other Sony models remain uncorrected (see `docs/sony-lens-corrections.md`); Nikon NEF distortion from NikonNEFInfo (`0xc7d5`: polynomial, frame-filling zoom and the optical-centre offset left by in-body stabilisation; `crates/raw/src/vendor/nef_lens.rs`, `docs/nikon-lens-corrections.md`) for the Z 6II, Z 8 and Z f, full-frame image area only: F-mount lenses on the FTZ match the camera JPEG within 0.2 px median, Z lenses within 0.5–1.5 px except the 24-70 f/4's wide end, where the camera applies a further correction that is not in the readable data (7 px median at 24 mm, from 16 px / 106 px p95 uncorrected); Fujifilm RAF raw-IFD tables (`0xf00b` distortion, `0xf010` vignetting; `crates/raw/src/vendor/raf_lens.rs`, `docs/fuji-lens-corrections.md`) become a `WarpRectilinear` with the camera's frame-filling zoom and a `FixVignetteRadial` gain for 59 body models with sample files checked against the camera JPEG (the table indexed by the source radius with last knot at the corner; on 67 body/lens combinations the corrected render's radial shape error against the camera JPEG falls from a median of 0.72 % to 0.05 % of the half diagonal, largest 0.42 %, and the vignetting gain of the JPEG tracks the table at a median correlation of 0.987); files whose table scale is not the recorded image's half diagonal (X-S20, X-T30 III, X-M5, X-T5 crops) and six unmatched bodies stay uncorrected; Fujifilm's lateral CA table (`0xf00f`) is real but is not applied, because our demosaic already registers the planes and the stored shift over-corrects; vignetting and lateral CA data of Olympus raws not read yet; Sony ARW shading, lateral CA and distortion tables (raw IFD `0x7031`–`0x7037`, with the encrypted `SR2SubIFD` copies as a checked fallback) are decoded with their camera settings, provenance and framing evidence for every body that records them (`crates/raw/src/lens_metadata.rs`), but only the ILCE-7RM4A and ILCE-7RM6 distortion above is applied: on the ILCE-7CR the unchanged distortion interpretation misses the camera JPEG at 24 mm (median 1.6 px, p95 6.2 px at 2376 px), and Sony shading and CA semantics are not validated beyond the ILCE-7RM6's (lateral CA off) (`docs/sony-lens-corrections.md`); new photos start with "Enable Profile Corrections" off, as in Lightroom Classic, except DNG, Panasonic / Leica RW2 / RWL and Fujifilm RAF files carrying their own correction, which start on (`Photo::lens_correction_built_in`; Lightroom applies those formats' built-in corrections whatever the switch says — inferred, not verified against Lightroom renders); a Canon CR3, Nikon NEF or Sony ARW, and any lens only the database matches, starts off, so browsing and culling never compute a warp; the photo's own XMP, a preset or the switch in Develop turns it on (`database_matched_raw_starts_with_profile_corrections_off`); Reload from Disk reads the file's lens data for photos catalogued without it and turns the correction on for those still at import settings when the format's starts on (issue #314); a lensfun-format lens database fills in for raws without their own correction (`crates/lensdb`, `crates/engine/src/lens_profiles.rs`; a smaller image area of a full-frame Nikon — 5:4, 1:1, 16:9, DX — gets the profile for its part of the sensor, placed by the maker note's CropHiSpeed window, `ImageArea`): Settings ▸ Import ▸ Lens profiles sets its folder or downloads the lensfun project's database on request (CC-BY-SA 3.0, never bundled; `cmd:lens.db.status`, `cmd:lens.db.setDir`, `cmd:lens.db.download`, `cmd:lens.db.remove`, `cmd:lens.db.refresh`, `cmd:lens.db.photo`, `cmd:app.lensDbFolder`), swapped at run time without a restart; Optics names what applies (the database profile, "(chosen)" for a given lens, or the file's own correction; issue #307) and why nothing applies (no database, with a link to that section: `cmd:app.settings` `section`; no lens named; no profile for the lens); corrections apply in a fixed order (the file's own, a lens given by the caller, the match by lens name), `cmd:lens.db.photo` `lens` reports `chosen` beside `embedded` / `found`, and `cmd:lens.db.list` lists the database's lenses by mount, crop factor and words: and a per-photo lens picker: Make → Model dropdowns from the database plus a focal-length field for photos that record none (`cmd:lens.choose`, stored in the develop settings' `optics.lens`, over the file's own correction; not written to XMP yet; issue #307). lensfun profiles are not verified against Lightroom renders |

| LR-PROF-CAMERACOLOR | Camera colour calibration (own) | P0 | 🟡 | `crates/raw/src/color.rs`, `crates/raw/src/profile.rs`, `crates/raw/src/gaintable.rs`, `crates/engine/src/camera_preview.rs` | DNG files use the colour matrices and the camera-profile look they carry (`ProfileHueSatMap`, `ProfileLookTable`, `ProfileToneCurve`, read from the file at run time per DNG spec ch. 6 — Lightroom-converted DNGs rendered flat and muted without them, issue #138). A DNG's `ProfileGainTableMap` / `ProfileGainTableMap2` (Apple ProRAW's local tone map; both versions, DNG 1.7 precedence, the file's byte order with a big-endian fallback) is read and written back by DNG export and not rendered by default, as Lightroom Classic renders ProRAW without it (measured on one CC0 iPhone 12 Pro ProRAW); an edit imported from Lightroom with the "Apple ProRaw" look keeps it (`Look/Parameters/ProfileGainTableMap` > 0 turns the option on, `crates/engine/src/crs.rs`, docs/xmp-interop.md); the Profile option "Camera local tone mapping" (`profile.camera_local_tone`, per photo, off by default, `crates/pipeline/src/local_tone.rs`) renders it as the camera does, per output pixel in the scene-linear stage (CPU, also under the GPU path); Sony ARW, Nikon NEF, Panasonic RW2, Fujifilm RAF, Canon CR3 and CR2, Pentax PEF, Samsung SRW and Olympus ORF get a guarded file-local fit to their own embedded JPEG — chromaticity matrix + hue/saturation/value table + tone and chroma curves, relative WB — or, with a bundled or local per-model profile pooled from many photos (`<cli> calibrate`, ARW, NEF, RAF, CR3, CR2, RW2, ORF, PEF, SRW and ERF, `crates/engine/src/camera_profiles.rs`; bundled Canon EOS R5 C from 398 photos, OneSlips), the profile's colour and only their own tone/chroma curves (on 29 held-out ILCE-7M4 photos mean ΔE vs the camera JPEG 3.54 → 3.10; bundled ILCE-7CR from 125 photos: 26 held-out 4.07 → 3.29, and every one of the 131 photos accepted, 8 of which fell back to neutral without it; bundled ILCA-99M2 from 144 photos: 30 held-out 5.11 → 4.87, 25 of them closer); the tone fit also uses dark JPEG samples excluded from colour training, so deep shadows follow the camera toe (#1307); Sony ARWs shot with DRO on (maker note `0xb025`, on by default) keep that colour but their tone curve is lowered to Sony's curve without DRO, whose lifted shadows are in the camera JPEG only (issue #244: 8 ILCE-7CR photos +6.4 → +0.8 L* from Lightroom's default, median ΔE 7.5 → 3.3); 52 models without a profile whose JPEG fit fails (or that have no usable JPEG) start from DNG-style matrices fitted to their measured spectral sensitivities instead of the neutral fallback (`crates/raw/src/spectral.rs`, rawtoaces-data v1.1.0, Apache-2.0, aliases included; not yet measured against the camera JPEG); ARW: on 10 public raw.pixls.us samples from 8 bodies 7 fits were accepted, mean ΔE vs the camera JPEG 17–26 → 3–10; NEF: 13 of 13 decodable samples (D750, D780, D850, D7500, Z 50 and local D7500 shots) accepted, ΔE 13–46 → 3–8 (issue #150); RW2 / RWL: 140 of 174 public samples accepted, from 93 of the 114 bodies with a preview, median ΔE 5.0 with matching lightness (13 of the 14 corpus files with a preview: ΔE 9.6–27 → 2.8–8.9; most rejections are compacts and kit zooms whose camera JPEG is distortion-corrected); a fit that fails the gates on all pixels is retried away from edges, where a lens-corrected camera JPEG still lines up (issue #232: the six distortion-corrected public ILCE-7RM2 samples ΔE 16.6–16.9 → 3.7–3.8, the corpus GX80 19.1 → 7.9); RAF: bundled X-H2S/X-T4 profiles from 201/545 photos; on 150 withheld files mean ΔE improves slightly over per-file fitting (X-H2S 1.70 → 1.62, X-T4 2.43 → 2.34); 23 of 24 references from 13 bodies accepted, including the dark X-H2S samples (8.74 → 2.25); ORF: the 3 decodable corpus files (E-1, E-400, XZ-2) accepted (`docs/camera-preview-colour.md`); a raw whose camera JPEG is black and white (a monochrome picture style) takes only the JPEG's tone curve, on the camera profile's, spectral or neutral colour (issue #577); this is not measured camera calibration; other raws and rejected fits use a neutral fallback (camera RGB ≈ linear sRGB, `matrix_is_fallback`) with as-shot white balance, so colours are muted and not accurate. Needs our own per-camera calibration: matrices the files carry themselves (e.g. Olympus `ColorMatrix`), fitting each camera to its own embedded JPEG, then chart shots. Adobe matrices / DCPs are never used. Biggest image-quality gap today; ILCE-7RM6: per-file fit now runs on previews above 64 MP, a pooled profile (46 photos) was tried and not bundled (withheld ΔE 5.71 → 6.40, 11 of 15 regressed), kept with smart previews (see docs/camera-preview-colour.md); the fit pairs the sensor moved into the file's lens-warp geometry when that lines up better with a lens-corrected camera JPEG (29 CC0 raws: alignment +0.13, mean ΔE 3.73 → 3.61); bundled NIKON Z 6_2 profile (296 photos; 59 held-back photos mean ΔE 2.73 → 2.54) NIKON Z 8 profile (261 photos; 55 held back 2.55 → 2.33) and NIKON D5300 profile (264 photos; 53 held back 2.86 → 2.66); bundled NIKON D780, D750 and D90 profiles (Rob Dunn's own photos, with a pooled tone curve that continues a dark photo's own curve when its exposure is pushed); a photo no look fits keeps its profile's colour, and raw decoding keeps negative channels so pushed shadows don't turn magenta (upstream #2092); colour-chart calibration: `<cli> calibrate-chart` fits a white-preserving forward and colour matrix per light (one or two) to raw shots of an X-Rite ColorChecker Classic and prints a `chart` camera-database entry and an optional `.dcp` (`crates/colourfit/src/chart.rs`, [chart-calibration](chart-calibration.md)); no camera measured yet |
| LR-PROF-CREATIVE | Creative profiles (own) | P2 | ✅ | `cmd:develop.profile`, `crates/pipeline/src/profiles.rs` | 16 own looks in Film / Cinematic / Muted / B&W (tone + point-curve fades, colour grading, mixer / B&W mix); scale with `ctl:profile.amount`; sliders untouched |
| LR-PROF-LEGACY | Legacy profiles | P2 | ⬜ | | |
| LR-PROF-NONRAW | Profiles for non-raw files | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:develop.profile` | same looks apply to JPEG/TIFF |
| LR-PROF-AMOUNT | Profile amount | P1 | ✅ | `ctl:profile.amount` | |
| LR-PROF-IMPORT | Import profiles | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:profile.import`, `cmd:profile.deleteImported`, `crates/pipeline/src/lut.rs`, `crates/engine/src/cmd/lut_profiles.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/edit.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/profiles.rs` | `.cube` 3D LUTs (files, folders, zips) become creative profiles with Amount, grouped in the profile browser, kept with the library; rendered on the CPU; Adobe profile formats deliberately unsupported; an XMP look (`crs:PresetType="Look"` with a colour table) imports like a `.cube` (`crates/engine/src/xmp_look.rs`); a `.dcp` among the paths goes to the camera profiles (as `camera.importDcp`) and is not also read as a LUT (OneSlips) |
| LR-PROF-CAMERACOLOR-CHOICE | Choose a raw's starting colour (camera look / calibrated) | P1 | 🟡 | `crates/develop/src/settings.rs` (`CameraColour`), `crates/engine/src/camera_preview.rs` (`colorimetric_camera`, `starting_colour`), `crates/engine/src/media.rs` (`LoadOptions`, `DecodedSource::serves`), `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/edit.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/tests_camera_colour.rs` | Profile ▸ Calibrated camera colour, for a raw without matrices whose model has spectral matrices in the camera database, else a row of dcraw's matrix table (`Source::Dcraw`, about 550 models): Auto (the camera-JPEG fit first) or Colorimetric (those matrices with the default tone curve, never the JPEG); part of the decode and of the Profile group (sync, presets, Raw Defaults); missing: a look of its own and exposure matching between the modes (upstream issue #1207) |
| LR-PROF-DCP | Import camera profiles (.dcp) | P2 | 🟡 | `cmd:camera.importDcp`, `cmd:camera.listDcp`, `cmd:camera.useDcp`, `cmd:camera.removeDcp`, `cmd:camera.exportDcp`, `cmd:develop.exportLut`, `cmd:develop.cameraProfile`, `crates/raw/src/dcp.rs`, `crates/engine/src/colour_export.rs`, `crates/engine/src/cmd/dcp_profiles.rs`, `crates/engine/src/camera_profiles.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/profiles.rs` | a person's own DNG camera profiles (files, folders or zips): a lone profile for a camera model becomes its default from the next start (a batch for one camera leaves the default as it is, issue #723), and the default camera profile per model (Settings ▸ Import, or `library.preferences` `cameraDefault`; `"*"` = every camera) is what photos start from when they pick none, ahead of the camera's active profile, and Profiles ▸ Camera Profile picks one per photo (part of the decode; a raw developed through a profile edits white balance in Kelvin and takes the profile's tone curve (Eboda Colour gives it the other app's default look); a profile without a tone curve of its own, as most made from a colour chart are, keeps the tone the photo has without it: its DNG tone curve, else the tone fitted to its camera JPEG, 2026-11-06); colour matrices, HueSatMap, LookTable and tone curve are used; none is shipped. Export as DNG writes the colour the photo renders with (its chosen `.dcp` or calibration look, else the starting colour) into the DNG (Convert to DNG, which replaces the original, keeps the camera's own colour); `camera.exportDcp` writes that colour as a `.dcp` for Lightroom, Camera Raw or RawTherapee, and `develop.exportLut` a photo's global look as a 33³ sRGB `.cube` (2026-10-26); both are in File ▸ More Exports (`file.exportCameraProfile`, `file.exportLookLut`); missing: per-profile previews |
| LR-PROF-BODY | Camera profile per camera body | P2 | 🟡 | `cmd:profiles.bodies`, `cmd:profile.setBodyDefault`, `cmd:profile.applyBodyDefaults`, `cmd:import.profilePlan`, `crates/engine/src/profile_bodies.rs` | a body (serial number, else the first two characters of its file names: `file:XX`) can have its own camera profile, applied to its raws at import while they are on the camera default (a sidecar that names a profile still wins) and to its photos in the library on request; set from Settings ▸ Profiles or the Profiles panel's "Make default for this body" (OneSlips); an import can choose one camera profile for its raws or one per body (`library.import cameraProfile`, import.profilePlan): the Import screen lists each camera body on the card with its own choice, and the import result counts the photos per profile |

Replace keeps the old file in `.replaced/` and is undoable. Not yet: tried on procedural charts only, no real camera measured |
| LR-PROF-MANAGE | Profile Manager | P2 | ✅ | `cmd:profiles.manager`, `cmd:profile.hide`, `cmd:profile.rename`, `cmd:profile.setGroup`, `cmd:profile.reorder`, `cmd:profile.remove`, `cmd:profile.restoreBuiltins`, `cmd:profile.groupRename`, `cmd:profile.groupHide`, `cmd:profile.groupRemove`, `crates/engine/src/profile_edits.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/settings_profiles.rs`, `cmd:dialog.profileManager`, `cmd:profile.deleteCamera`, `cmd:profile.deleteImported`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/profile_manager.rs` | Settings ▸ Profiles (also Manage Profiles… in the profile menu): hide a profile from the menu and browser (photos using it keep it; `profiles.list {all}` still lists it), rename it, move it to a group of your own, reorder, remove built-in looks (Restore Built-in Looks brings them back), and the same for a whole group; the camera bodies' profiles (OneSlips). The Profile Manager window (Photo ▸ Develop Settings ▸ Manage Profiles…, the profile browser's Manage…, Settings ▸ Profiles) shows it all in the Presets Manager's table: the user's camera profiles by camera model, LUT groups, built-in looks, bundled camera profiles; Hide / Show (camera profiles too: out of the camera profile choices), Rename and Group (looks and LUTs), Favorite, Import Profiles… / Import Folder…, Delete of the user's own .dcp and LUT files asked in the row (built-ins hidden, not deleted), photo counts and body defaults per row; fits the screen and keeps its size; the browser's None (camera profile only) in one click (from OneSlips). Not yet: dropping files on the window, drag-reordering of groups |

## H. Crop & rotate (CROP)

| Id | Feature | Tier | Status | Evidence | Missing / notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LR-CROP-RECT | Crop rectangle | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:crop.set`, `cmd:crop.drag`, `crates/geom/src/crop_drag.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/detail.rs` | double-click inside the box applies the crop (like Return); on a straightened photo a moved box slides along the tilted edges to where the pointer asks, so a box that fills the photo's height still reaches its sides (issue #2299) |
| LR-CROP-ASPECT | Aspect ratios | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:crop.aspect`, `cmd:crop.rotateAspect`, `cmd:crop.aspects`, `cmd:crop.saveAspect`, `cmd:crop.deleteAspect`, `cmd:crop.moveAspect`, `cmd:crop.hideAspect`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/right.rs`, `crates/engine/src/cmd/crop_aspects.rs` | menu with presets + custom W × H; your own ratios saved under a name from the Custom row (any number of them, not Lightroom's five unnamed ones; the aspect button shows the name), and Edit List in the menu reorders and deletes them and hides built-in ratios (Free and Original always show), kept in the library's prefs.json (issue #1854); visible Lock toggle (also A, its own row in Keyboard Shortcuts: `crop.lockAspect` toggles it, sharing A with Visualize Spots by context), button names the active ratio; every handle honours the lock, holding Shift while dragging a handle keeps the frame's proportions for that drag (issue #614), and the drag stops at the image edge instead of shifting the frame (issue #295); "Original" is the photo's own shape (no separate As Shot); the last chosen aspect is saved as a crop tool default, resolving Original per photo and preserving existing crops; opening Crop or browsing never edits a photo: the default applies only when a crop gesture starts, within its single undo step |
| LR-CROP-STRAIGHTEN | Straighten tool | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:crop.straighten`, `cmd:crop.autoStraighten`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/detail.rs` (`rotate_cursor`, `angle_readout`), `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/right.rs` (Angle field), `crates/ui-egui/src/tests_crop_rotate.rs` | Straighten Tool button: drag along a horizon/vertical; double-click or Auto levels automatically. Dragging outside the crop box rotates it: there the pointer is a curved double arrow that bulges away from the side of the box it is on, diagonally off a corner and following a rotated box (`rotate_cursor_turn`, tested by `rotate_cursor_faces_away_from_the_crop_box`) (with ⌘ held, the straighten crosshair), and while rotating the angle shows next to it, kept on the canvas (issue #534); an exact angle goes in the Angle field under Straighten (`cropAngleField`: applies on Return or leaving it, Esc keeps the old angle, takes a decimal comma; a drag on it is one undo step) or by clicking the Straighten value (its hover says so). Every way of setting the angle (rotate handle, Straighten slider, Angle field, `crop.straighten`, `crop.set {angle}`, and the generic `develop.set` / `develop.adjust` / `develop.quickAdjust` / `develop.resetControl` on `crop.angle`, as MCP, the control channel and `<cli> render --set` use them) keeps the crop if it still fits, else shrinks it about its own centre, keeping its aspect, until it fits; the full photo (or a crop an earlier turn shrank to the largest of its aspect) stays the largest, so it grows back towards 0° (issue #1388), and no other crop ever grows (issue #1794: a crop dragged in from a corner turned into nearly the whole photo) (`CropGeometry::with_angle`, `crates/engine/src/tests_crop_angle.rs`; before issue #742 the generic path left the box, and the export, past the photo with smeared edge pixels in the corners). The dimming outside the box is the photo minus the (rotated) box, drawn as one mesh (`outside_crop_mesh`), so rotating never dims wedges inside the box or doubles them outside (issue #731). As in Lightroom the box stays upright on screen and the photo turns under it; while straightening, rotating or moving, the box keeps its place and the photo turns or slides beneath it (`CropLayout`, `crates/ui-egui/src/tests_crop_frame.rs`), and the uncropped view isn't re-rendered for a crop or angle change (fork issue #60) |
| LR-CROP-AUTO | Auto straighten | P1 | 🟡 | `cmd:crop.autoStraighten` | crop-angle leveling from detected horizon/plumb lines (consensus required); turns by at most 10° (`STRAIGHTEN_MAX_DEGREES`) and only on at least 3 agreeing lines (`STRAIGHTEN_MIN_LINES`), and says so when it finds nothing (`auto_straighten_says_when_it_finds_nothing`). Measured on 68 photos straightened by hand in Lightroom Classic and 300 left as shot: it levels 14 of the 68 within 0.5° (1 more than 1° off) and turns 19 of the 300 by more than 1° (before the limits: 79, some by 20° or more). 🟡 because it still leaves most tilted photos alone: a soft sea horizon among wave crests, and verticals that converge in perspective, aren't used yet |
| LR-CROP-ANGLE | Angle slider | P0 | ✅ | `ctl:crop.angle`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/right.rs` | the Straighten slider; an exact angle can also be typed in the Angle field under it (see LR-CROP-STRAIGHTEN); setting `crop.angle` through `develop.set` fits the crop inside the photo as Straighten does (issue #742) |
| LR-GUIDED-SELECT | Guide selection in Guided Upright mode | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:tool.guidedUpright`, `cmd:geometry.guides`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/detail.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/state.rs` (`selected_guide`), `crates/ui-egui/src/tests_guided_guides.rs` | a press near a guide's line selects it (`selected_guide = Some((photo id, index))`; another press on it deselects), a press on an empty spot starts a new guide at that point, and an endpoint drags to reshape the guide (`Gesture::GuideEndpoint`, one undo step; clicking an endpoint only selects); tested in `tests_guided_guides` 8/8 |
| LR-GUIDED-DELETE | Delete removes the selected guide alone | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:photo.delete`, `crates/ui-egui/src/shortcuts.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/tests_guided_guides.rs` | with the Guided Upright tool open (`RightPanel::Crop && tool == "guidedUpright"`), Delete / Backspace removes only the selected guide and clears the selection; with nothing selected it removes nothing (the photo itself is never deleted while the tool is open); tested in `tests_guided_guides` |
| LR-CROP-ROTATE90 | Rotate 90° | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:photo.rotateLeft`, `cmd:photo.rotateRight` | |
| LR-CROP-FLIP | Flip | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:photo.flipHorizontal`, `cmd:photo.flipVertical`, `crates/pipeline/src/geometry.rs` | the crop tool's uncropped view shows the flips too, with the crop box and its dimming mirrored to match |
| LR-CROP-OVERLAY | Crop overlays | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:view.cropOverlay`, `cmd:view.cropOverlayOrientation`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/crop_overlay.rs` | thirds, grid, golden ratio, diagonal, triangle, golden spiral (mirrored with ⇧O while cropping); no aspect-ratio overlays; Crop Mask strength Off/20/50/60/80/100 % (`cmd:view.cropMaskOpacity`) |
| LR-CROP-ZOOM | Zoom while cropping | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:view.zoom100`, `cmd:view.zoomIn`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/detail.rs` | zoom levels apply with the crop tool open (verified: 100% shows the photo at native size with the crop frame) |

| LR-MASK-OBJECTS | Object selection | P2 | 🟡 | `cmd:mask.add` (`object`, `prompt`), `cmd:mask.objectPoint`, `cmd:mask.refineDetail`, `cmd:segment.prepare`, `cmd:segment.model.status`, `cmd:segment.model.download`, `cmd:segment.model.cancel`, `cmd:app.openSamModelFolder`, `cmd:ai.models.status`, `cmd:ai.models.download`, `cmd:ai.models.setObjectModel`, `cmd:dialog.aiModels`, `crates/segment`, `crates/engine/src/segment/mod.rs` | SAM 3 in pure Rust (candle; Metal on macOS, CPU elsewhere): Object tile → click to include, ⌥-click to leave out; Describe tile → a text prompt selects every instance ("sky", "the red car"); both also as Add/Subtract/Intersect components; + / − under the selected mask; comma lists (`car, road`); a zoomed-in detail pass for 5–10× finer edges on small objects; per-selection Edge (hard ↔ soft); the stored grid is enlarged along the photo's edges (a guided filter of its logits by log luminance, so no outline spills onto the background under a raised Exposure; People and remembered-object grids are still enlarged plainly). The model runs on its own worker thread (the UI never waits; panics become errors), is unloaded after 10 min idle, and is optional: the segmentation is stored with the mask (288² logits), so renders and exports never need it. Weights are never bundled (SAM License): without them the app offers a consented background download (mirrors, resume, timeouts, SHA-256) — **but no default download location is configured yet** (users set the `<ENV_PREFIX>_SAM3_MIRRORS` variable or install by hand: the dialog names the three files, opens the configured model folder with the host opener, preserves setup on an open error, and links docs/ai-masks.md; Meta's original checkpoint installed in place of the Hugging Face one is named as such). Object clicks can also run on SAM 2.1 Hiera-Tiny (Apache-2.0, downloaded on request from Meta's Hugging Face repository, pinned; ported by OneSlips; `ai.models.setObjectModel`, AI Models dialog; Automatic keeps SAM 3 when installed), so Object masks no longer need the gated model; Describe still does. Not verified against Lightroom's Select Object; no brush/box object mode; the first click on a photo waits for its analysis (~4 s on an M4 Pro, much longer on CPU) |

| LR-MASK-BRUSH | Brush mask | P0 | 🟡 | `cmd:tool.brush`, `cmd:mask.brushStroke` (`autoMask`), `cmd:brush.preset`, `crates/pipeline/src/masks.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/brush_live.rs`, `cmd:mask.editComponent` | size/feather/flow/density/erase; Lightroom Classic's brush model (ported from OneSlips; `crates/develop/src/brush_model.rs`): Size is the solid core, Feather a soft halo outside it (up to 2.5 × Size), each dab `exp(−(r/a)^p)` black-box-fitted to Lightroom exports of a procedural chart, dabs every 0.3 × radius, overlapping strokes add up instead of taking the maximum (CPU, GPU and the live stroke agree); strokes saved before keep the old model; Lightroom-model Auto Mask uses tolerances measured on Lightroom (0.7–0.8 EV cut, chroma 0.17–0.42, pixel-sharp edge) and an Auto Mask range slider (−100 … +100 scales them ½ … 2, kept with each stroke, `autoRange`); Create New Mask starts a separate brush in paint mode; the stroke shows while the button is held with the committed stroke's dab spacing, feather and flow, so fast strokes have no gaps (#517); coverage builds up by passes, not hold time (holding the brush still adds nothing, live or committed, #518); Auto Mask: dabs weighted by similarity to the colour under the dab centre, refined by a guided filter on luminance (CPU + GPU); ⇧-click draws a straight stroke from where the last stroke ended (`connect`), tapering size, feather and flow from that stroke's values when they changed; A / B brush presets (`brush.preset`, panel A / B buttons), each remembering size, feather, flow and density (saved with the UI preferences); pen pressure (Brush ▸ Pen Pressure, on by default; `pressure` per point in `mask.brushStroke`) scales each dab's size (a tenth to all of it) and flow, live and committed alike, where winit reports a pen force (Windows; not macOS / Linux tablets yet); no tilt; a plain mouse-wheel notch over the photo resizes the brush like `[` / `]` (Preferences → Interface, on by default; fork #96); a zoomed window with a large brushed mask renders in ~150 ms: the GPU kernel bins each stroke's dabs into tiles and the CPU stamps only the dabs that reach the window (OneSlips); Brush / Linear / Radial arm and make their mask on the first stroke or drag (one undo step); a selected mask, another photo or leaving Masking puts an armed tool away; a component row edits that component (a Brush arms the brush on it); ⌥ read through a stroke (runs of one mode); a mask with settings shows its stroke live; the eraser has its own Size / Feather / Flow / Density; Size is a 1–100 screen size (older photo-fraction sizes migrate); cross / minus at the brush centre |

## O. Merge (MERGE)

| Id | Feature | Tier | Status | Evidence | Missing / notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LR-MERGE-HDR | HDR merge | P2 | 🟡 | `cmd:merge.hdr`, `cmd:dialog.mergeHdr`, `crates/merge/src/frame.rs` (`Frame::lens`), `crates/merge/src/hdr.rs`, `crates/merge/src/refine.rs` | auto align (refined at full size to ~0.1 px; local shifts such as parallax corrected tile by tile, `crates/merge/src/flow.rs`; the finest detail from the reference where it is well exposed; a mostly clipped frame the small versions can't align is recovered; Lanczos-3 warp; `tests/sharpness.rs`: 98.8 % of a handheld scene's detail kept, 86 % before), deghost None–High + overlay, click-to-zoom preview (drag pans; the zoom stays while options change), auto settings, float DNG, Create Stack; the DNG keeps the reference frame's embedded lens corrections (tested by `hdr_merge_keeps_the_reference_frames_lens_corrections`); raws without colour matrices of their own (CR2, NEF, ARW…) keep the colour they open with, written as DNG tags (`starting_colour_tags`; the look's chroma curve has no tag; panoramas too), issue #965; the DNG's `AsShotNeutral` is the median of the inputs' vendor white-balance multipliers, issue #1144. Tested on real Sony A7R VI brackets (67 MP, 2026-10-14): frames agree with the merged radiance within ~1 % in all channels, exposure steps match EXIF within 0.03 EV. A clipped highlight of the reference that moved in other frames (a star at 200 mm) stays instead of becoming a black hole, and deghost (≤ 2048 px maps) catches a brighter frame's ghost in a dark sky (`a_clipped_star_of_the_reference_is_not_erased_by_frames_where_it_moved`, `deghost_removes_a_bright_frames_ghost_in_a_dark_sky`). JPEG / TIFF brackets are linearised through a camera response curve recovered from the bracket (Debevec–Malik, `crates/merge/src/response.rs`; `a_jpeg_bracket_through_a_known_gamma_merges_to_linear_radiance`), and frames are held as half floats (`crates/merge/src/half.rs`, 8 instead of 16 bytes a pixel with the saturation map; a 5 × 48 MP merge peaks at 3.6 GB, was 5.1 GB; `the_half_float_merge_agrees_with_the_f32_mean`). Merge to Layers (issue #769, `merge.hdr {layers: true}`, dialog ▸ Also Write Aligned Layers): a layered 8/16-bit PSD/PSB of the frames as the merge aligned them (global fit, refinement, local flow), transparent where the warp left the frame, named with their EV steps, optionally the merged result and the deghost map as a hidden layer, stacked under the DNG (`crates/engine/src/merge_layers.rs`, `hdr_merge_writes_aligned_layers`, `aligned_frames_line_up_with_the_reference`); layers carry no lens corrections, no layered TIFF, no open-in-editor step yet. 🟡 because: AI Denoise doesn't run on merged DNGs (Bayer only; Lightroom's does); gap 29 |
| LR-MERGE-PANO | Panorama | P2 | ✅ | `cmd:merge.panorama`, `cmd:dialog.mergePanorama` | spherical/cylindrical/perspective + auto (projected with Lanczos-3), boundary warp, auto crop, fill edges (diffusion), DNG whose `AsShotNeutral` is the median of its frames' as-shot white balance (vendor multipliers for CR2, NEF, ARW…), issue #1144; no lens model / 360° wrap |
| LR-MERGE-HDRPANO | HDR panorama | P2 | ✅ | `cmd:merge.hdrPanorama`, `cmd:dialog.mergeHdrPanorama` | brackets grouped by EXIF; merged one bracket at a time; previews reuse decoded photos when an option changes (`crates/engine/src/merge.rs` `FrameCache`); 12 × 48 MP DJI DNGs preview in 1.9 s / 1.9 GB |
| LR-MERGE-FOCUS | Focus stack (beyond Lightroom) | P2 | 🟡 | `cmd:merge.focusStack`, `cmd:dialog.mergeFocusStack`, `crates/merge/src/focus.rs`, `crates/merge/tests/focus.rs` | photos focused at different distances → one DNG sharp throughout (`<name>-Focus.dng`), the photos taken in shooting order (capture time, then name): neighbour-to-neighbour similarity alignment chained (focus breathing) and refined at full size against the aligned neighbour (0.03 px on `tests/focus_sharpness.rs`, up to 1.9 px before), Lanczos-3 resampling (98–99 % of an ideal stack's texture), a failed step bridged to one of the three frames before, frames that align to nothing left out and reported; the most magnified frame as the reference; brightness measured from the pixels (auto ISO), EXIF only as a fallback; Laplacian-pyramid fusion with a steep soft selection above the noise floor, so out-of-focus areas average (about 5× less noise than one frame on a real 88-frame A7R VI macro crop) while texture keeps 95–100 % of a maximum selection's; one full-size frame in memory at a time; auto align, auto settings, Create Stack; type filter "Focus Stacks". Checked on a 175-frame A7R VI stack (no ghosting) and synthetic brackets; a similarity only (no tilt or rotation about the lens's side); no retouching between frames, no depth-map mode, no halo control. Not a Lightroom feature (Photoshop's Auto-Blend Layers ▸ Stack Images) |
| LR-MERGE-HEADLESS | Merge with last settings | P2 | ✅ | `cmd:merge.hdrLast`, `cmd:merge.panoramaLast`, `cmd:merge.hdrPanoramaLast`, `cmd:merge.hdrBatch`, `crates/catalog/src/stacks.rs` (`bracket_sets`) | no dialog; the options of the last merge of that kind (defaults the first time). HDR for Each Bracket Set merges a batch like Lightroom's merge of stacks: one HDR per stack among the photos, then per bracket (photos taken less than a second apart, after the shutter time, at exposures ≥ ½ EV apart; bursts at one exposure are skipped), one after the other in the activity stack (cancelling one stops the rest; a failed set doesn't); CLI `merge hdr-batch FILES…` |

## S. Export (EXP)

| Id | Feature | Tier | Status | Evidence | Missing / notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LR-EXP-DIALOG | Export dialog | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:dialog.export`, `cmd:app.export` (`preset`), `cmd:export.presets`, `cmd:export.savePreset`, `cmd:export.deletePreset`, `cmd:export.renamePreset`, `cmd:export.importPresets`, `cmd:export.exportPresets`, `cmd:file.exportExportPresets`, `crates/engine/src/cmd/export_preset_files.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/dialogs.rs`, `crates/engine/src/export.rs` | batch export; user presets keep format, quality, file-size limit, sizing, sharpening, file naming, destination folder (`dir`) and subfolder, metadata and watermark settings, can be renamed, exported (`.lcexport`) and imported (`.lcexport`, Lightroom Classic export `.lrtemplate`: settings without a counterpart, such as its watermark or post-processing, named in the report; gap 36), from the preset column's Rename / Import… / Export…; built-in presets (JPEG Small 2048 px / Large full size, Original + Settings, DNG, Instagram Feed 1080 px, Instagram Story / Reel 1920 px, Web / Portfolio 2048 px copyright-only metadata, Full Resolution Master full size P3) and saved user presets (library prefs) load into the dialog; laid out like Lightroom Classic's: the presets in a column on the left (click to load, keeping where to export unless the preset says; Add / Remove for user presets; a Preset menu instead in a narrow window), the settings in its order (Export Location, File Naming, Video, File Settings, Image Sizing, Output Sharpening, Metadata, Watermarking, Post-Processing), each section folding from a header that summarises it (Export Location, File Naming, File Settings start open; Image Sizing, Output Sharpening, Metadata, Watermarking folded; the choice is kept, `groupOpen` `export:<section>`); the window opens wider (900 pt) and can be dragged wider, for a large UI font; slider names sit in the form's label column (`widgets::form_slider`); tested by `export_dialog_sections_fold_and_stay_folded`, `export_screen_shows_every_section_in_order_and_its_rail_jumps_to_one`; on a short window the options scroll and Cancel / OK stay visible (LR-BEHAV-DIALOGS, issue #781); a cancelled export stops within about a second (the photos in flight stop at their next step, `run_batch_cancellable`), Limit file size takes at most two full encodes (quality predicted from a mosaic of the photo, `fit_jpeg`), and a gain map JPEG develops its photo once and encodes its gain map once (OneSlips' port); an export asked for while another runs waits its turn (a "Waiting to export" row in the activity stack, ✕ drops it) instead of being refused (`export_task::Queued`, issue #2965) |
| LR-EXP-TYPE | File types | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:app.export` (`format`), `crates/engine/src/export.rs` (`ExportFormat`), `crates/engine/src/tests_export.rs` | JPEG, PNG, TIFF, PSD (flat, 8/16-bit, no layers: `crates/codecs/src/psd.rs`), WebP, AVIF, JPEG XL (issue #247: `jixel`, native only; lossy with quality or lossless, 8/16-bit, effort fast/normal (jixel's slowest search left out: jxl-oxide misdecodes its files), progressive, EXIF/XMP boxes; lossy files in Adobe RGB / ProPhoto are written as Rec. 2020, lossless keep any space via ICC; `crates/codecs/src/jxl_encode.rs`), DNG (raw photos: lossless re-encode with the edits in the embedded XMP; a raw without colour matrices of its own, as a NEF, gets the colour it opens with as `ColorMatrix1` / `ForwardMatrix1` / `AsShotNeutral`, also in Convert / Copy as DNG, so other raw converters open it, issue #1745), Original (+ XMP sidecar). Non-raw → DNG not supported; no JPEG XL export in the web build. A transparent source keeps its alpha in PNG and WebP (straight) and TIFF (associated, `ExtraSamples` = 1), at 8 and 16 bits and in 32-bit float TIFF: its colour is developed without the white it is shown on, and the alpha follows orientation, crop, straighten, flips, lens and Transform corrections and resizing, with areas a warp brings in from outside the photo transparent; a watermark over transparent pixels raises their alpha (upstream #3116, `export_job_with_alpha` in `crates/engine/src/media.rs`, `render_alpha` in `crates/pipeline/src/lib.rs`; tested by `transparent_png_export_keeps_its_alpha` in `apps/cli/tests/cli.rs` and `transparent_sources_keep_their_alpha_in_png_tiff_and_webp` / `exported_alpha_follows_rotate_crop_and_resize` in `crates/engine/src/tests_export.rs`). JPEG, PSD, AVIF, JPEG XL and HDR exports, and an offline original exported from its smart preview, composite it onto white. |
| LR-EXP-DIM | Output size | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:app.export` (`longEdge`, `shortEdge`, `width`, `height`, `fit`, `megapixels`, `percent`, `dontEnlarge`, `ppi`), `crates/engine/src/export.rs` (`Resize`), `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/dialogs.rs` | full size = the cropped native size (no longer upscaled); both of Lightroom's boxes: Dimensions (W × H fits either orientation) and Width & Height (`fit: widthHeight`, each edge bound to its own number, #1620); ppi written to JFIF / pHYs / TIFF tags; at most 65,535 px an edge, and an export enlarged past the photo's size stops at 512 megapixels (Lightroom's ceiling too) and is refused, with the size it would need, when it needs more memory than the computer has (it aborted the process before) |
| LR-EXP-QUALITY | JPEG quality | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:app.export` (`quality`, `limitKb`), `crates/codecs/src/jpeg_par.rs` | 4:4:4 chroma (4:2:0 when a file-size limit is set); Huffman tables made for each image from its own symbol counts (T.81 Annex K.2) instead of the example tables: the same pixels, 12–14 % smaller files at quality 80–100 on a 24 MP photo-like image, still interleaved baseline (issue #1373); ordinary and HDR gain map JPEGs search quality up to the requested maximum to fit `limitKb`, including gain map and metadata, or return an error. Tested by `size_limit_respected` and `hdr_jpeg_size_limit_respected`.; the Export dialog notes what a size limit does and warns when it is tight for the largest photo at the chosen size (`size_limit_is_tight`) |
| LR-EXP-BITDEPTH | Bit depth | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:app.export` (`bitDepth`), `crates/pipeline/src/output.rs` (`OutputDepth`), `crates/engine/src/export.rs` | 16-bit PNG/TIFF/JPEG XL rendered at 16 bits (TIFF defaults to 16), 32-bit float linear TIFF with a linear profile, 10-bit AVIF; high-bit-depth renders run on the CPU |
| LR-EXP-COMPRESSION | TIFF compression | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:app.export` (`tiffCompression`: none / lzw / zip) | |
| LR-EXP-COLORSPACE | Output colour space | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:app.export` (`colorSpace`), `crates/pipeline/src/output.rs`, `crates/engine/src/export.rs` | sRGB, Display P3, Adobe RGB (1998) compatible, ProPhoto RGB, Rec. 2020: rendered from the working space with gamut mapping into the target gamut (CPU + GPU), own ICC profile embedded; AVIF stays sRGB (muxer has no ICC); lossy JPEG XL declares sRGB / P3 / Rec. 2020 (Adobe RGB and ProPhoto written as Rec. 2020), lossless JPEG XL embeds the profile |
| LR-EXP-HDR | HDR output | P2 | 🟡 | `cmd:app.export`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/dialogs.rs` | HDR output checkbox in the export dialog: gain map JPEG, PQ or HLG AVIF, PQ PNG, PQ JPEG XL, float TIFF; no HDR presets yet |
| LR-EXP-SHARPEN | Output sharpening | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:app.export` (`sharpen`, `sharpenAmount`) | |
| LR-EXP-METADATA | Metadata policy | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:app.export` (`metadata`, `removeLocation`) | remove location: an original copied byte for byte is refused when it embeds GPS anywhere it can be read (`lcc_meta::embeds_location`; for HEIF / HEIC / AVIF any EXIF or XMP item, a thumbnail's too, and an unreadable one counts), with JPEG or TIFF offered instead, and DNG for raws |
| LR-EXP-WATERMARK | Watermark | P1 | 🟡 | `cmd:app.export` (`watermark`: text, or `{text, vertical, size, opacity, anchor, insetX, insetY, inset, rotation, color, shadow, image, imageWidth}`), `crates/engine/src/export.rs` (`Watermark`, `WATERMARK_PARAMS`) | text uses Inter SemiBold, the existing Mincho-first CJK chain, then Thai faces from `CRAFT_FONTS_DIR`, shared with contact-sheet captions and slideshow overlays (#2052, partial). Decomposed (NFD) text, as macOS often writes file names, is composed to NFC first, so Hangul syllables and Vietnamese letters draw whole instead of as loose jamo and marks; CJK compatibility ideographs keep their code points (`decomposed_hangul_draws_whole_syllables`, `decomposed_vietnamese_draws_its_composed_letters`, `compatibility_ideographs_keep_their_code_points`). Horizontal Thai mark positioning remains incomplete. Arabic export fallback is deferred until joining and bidi reordering exist; UI font fallback is unchanged. Text (`size` = height as a fraction of the short edge, 0.005..0.5, default 0.035; `color` sRGB `[r,g,b]`; `shadow`; Japanese vertical glyph forms and origins, tested by `biz_ud_vertical_watermarks_place_punctuation_at_the_top_right`; extended grapheme cells keep decomposed dakuten/handakuten and Latin accents together, tested by `vertical_watermarks_keep_combining_marks_in_one_cell` and `upright_watermark_cell_retains_multiple_glyphs` (see `docs/vertical-watermarks.md`)) or a graphic with transparency (EXIF orientation applied before sizing; oversized graphics fit the rotated image bounds without stretching, tested by `graphic_watermark_honours_exif_orientation` and `graphic_watermark_keeps_aspect_when_fitted_and_rotated`; `image` path, `imageWidth` = fraction of the photo's width, 0.01..1, converted to the output colour space); `anchor` (nine positions), `insetX` / `insetY` (margins from the side and from the top/bottom edges, each a fraction of the short edge, 0..0.4; the Export dialog's Horizontal and Vertical inset sliders, as in Lightroom Classic; the older single `inset` still reads as both; tested by `watermark_insets_are_horizontal_and_vertical`), `rotation` (0, 90, 180 or 270 degrees clockwise, the Export dialog's Rotate row, as Lightroom Classic's rotate buttons; sized before it is turned, placed and inset by the turned box; tested by `watermark_rotation_turns_the_text_in_its_corner` and `graphic_watermark_is_placed_scaled_and_blended`), `opacity` 0..1. Unknown keys and out-of-range values are errors (issue #183). The Export dialog previews it on the first exported photo's thumbnail (`draw_watermark_preview` scales the 6 px minimum text size to the preview so it matches the file), tested by `watermark_preview_scales_the_minimum_text_size` and `export_dialog_previews_the_watermark` |

Tested end to end with the packaged app on macOS 26.6.2 / Photos 11.0 (manual steps in `docs/apple-photos.md`). Other platforms' photo libraries are not covered |

Imported PSD/PSB photos retain embedded capture dates; PSD edit copies retain EXIF/XMP fields in the catalog and All Metadata. Original with Remove Location Info is refused when embedded PSD/PSB GPS is detected. An HDR merge can also write its aligned frames as a layered PSD/PSB (LR-MERGE-HDR, Merge to Layers). Not yet verified against a PhotoCraft build with events / `--wait` (feature-detected) |

Photo ▸ File ▸ Render to DNG writes `<name>-Rendered.dng` next to each selected original (never overwriting), adds it to the library stacked on top of the original and selects the new photos; a batch shows in the activity stack and can be cancelled between photos (copies made so far stay); failures in a batch are listed and the rest carry on |

| LR-PREF-PEOPLE | Face recognition | P2 | 🟡 | `cmd:faces.enable`, `cmd:faces.models.list`, `cmd:faces.models.inspect`, `cmd:faces.models.install`, `cmd:faces.models.download`, `cmd:faces.models.downloads`, `cmd:faces.models.downloadCancel`, `cmd:faces.models.remove`, `cmd:faces.models.select`, `cmd:dialog.faceModel`, `crates/faces/src/lib.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/faces.rs` | Settings ▸ Faces: an off-by-default toggle and the face model list. Models are opt-in and never bundled, the 232 KB YuNet detector included. Press Download (terms first; then a pinned address, size and SHA-256 checked, fetched over pure-Rust https with `lcc-fetch`), or add any `.onnx` by picking or dropping it: it is recognised by hash or described from its shape, shows its licence terms and training-data note, runs a self-test, and installs only after an "I accept" box; an installed model is chosen and recognition switched on at once (the newest wins, each model keeps its own embedding cache). Non-commercial models are add-by-file only, never offered for download. Suggestions of names run on the shared pure-Rust CPU interpreter (`crates/faces/src/net.rs`, `crates/faces/src/runtime.rs`; bounded im2col/GEMM, checked ONNX shapes/operators; no external inference runtime; 40 public crops agree within 2.1e-6, one-thread SFace 34–36 ms / AuraFace 233–252 ms; the whole network runs on the GPU through wgpu, activations kept on the device, when a background check (shown in the activity stack) finds it agrees with the CPU and is faster: YuNet ~3×, SFace ~4×, AuraFace ~4× faster; face detection (MediaPipe BlazeFace + FaceMesh, `crates/tflite/src/device.rs`) is lowered to the same device program and runs there under the same check, a photo's detector windows batched; Settings ▸ Faces shows the device and can keep them on the CPU (`cmd:faces.gpu`); the scan's pace limits GPU use, see docs/faces.md) in a background worker and are never applied automatically. Thresholds are starting points calibrated on busts, not yet on live faces; the Recognise faces switch is off until a recognition model is chosen and installed (`faces.enable {enabled: true}` refuses otherwise; removing the last recogniser switches it off), issue #678 |
| LR-PREF-WATERMARK | Watermark settings | P1 | ✅ | `cmd:export.savePreset`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/dialogs.rs` | set in the Export dialog; kept with Export with Previous and in saved export presets |

Rating/label digits and secondary keys are listed read-only and yield to a key given to a command |

## X. Cross-cutting behaviours (BEHAV)

| Id | Feature | Tier | Status | Evidence | Missing / notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LR-BEHAV-AUTOSAVE | Instant autosave | P0 | ✅ | `crates/catalog/src/journal.rs`, `crates/engine/src/library.rs` | every change fsynced before the command returns; a failed write fails the command ("saved in memory but not written to disk"), keeps the change queued and retries; top-bar warning until saved; a write that fails part-way is cut back to the last whole record so the retry never reads back as damage (logs from older builds with such a fragment load in full); quitting saves queued changes in the closing snapshot even when the log can't be appended to; versioned catalog format (`journal::VERSION`): older libraries are upgraded on open, a library from a newer version of the app is refused untouched ("written by a newer version"); a damaged or hand-edited snapshot that still reads is made consistent on open (`Catalog::repair`: records filed under another id, stacks of missing, repeated or too few photos, folders inside themselves, id counters behind the ids in use) instead of panicking or hanging later |
| LR-BEHAV-UNDO | Global undo | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:edit.undo`, `crates/engine/src/tests.rs` (`rating_flag_undo_redo`), `crates/engine/src/tests_filesystem_history.rs` | covers ratings, albums, deletes, edits; partial file/XMP moves keep unfinished repairs across retries and discarded history; merged renames move directly to their final catalog path |
| LR-BEHAV-MULTISELECT | Multi-selection | P0 | ✅ | `cmd:library.select` (`replace`/`add`/`toggle`/`range`), `cmd:library.selectAll` | |

| LR-BEHAV-PREVIEW-HOVER | Hover previews | P1 | ✅ | `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/presets.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/profiles.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/right.rs` (`versions`), `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/detail.rs` | presets, profile menu, profile browser and versions preview in the loupe |
| LR-BEHAV-PROGRESSIVE | Progressive rendering | P0 | ✅ | `crates/engine/src/media.rs`, `crates/preview/src/lib.rs` | |
| LR-BEHAV-BG-TASKS | Background tasks | P0 | ✅ | `crates/preview/src/lib.rs` (`JobPool`), `crates/ui-egui/src/export_task.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/import.rs` | renders off the UI thread; exports started from the UI run on a worker thread with a progress panel and Cancel (`ui.inspect` → `export`), several photos of a batch side by side (`export_parallelism`, memory-gated; files, names and order as one at a time, #496); imports show a progress window. No combined activity centre |
| LR-BEHAV-OFFLINE | Offline editing | P1 | ✅ | | local-first: everything works offline |
| LR-BEHAV-WEB-SAFETY | Library safety in the browser build | P1 | 🟡 | `cmd:file.backupLibrary`, `cmd:file.restoreLibrary`, `apps/web/src/safety.rs`, `apps/web/src/backup.rs`, `apps/web/src/files.rs` | web only (experimental): Back Up Catalog (zip of the catalog and every stored original) and a non-destructive Restore; failed saves show as unsaved and are retried; a photo that can't be stored isn't added; one tab per library (Web Locks); notices for storage that isn't persistent and for a library that can't open; `?reset` asks first; a panic shows a message. Missing: `?safe` start, deleting originals of removed photos, zip64 (backups > 4 GB) |
| LR-BEHAV-GPU | GPU acceleration | P0 | ✅ | `crates/gpu/src/render.rs`, `cmd:app.gpu`, `docs/gpu-pipeline.md` | CPU fallback per render on device limits, out of memory, device errors / loss, incomplete or black results (`perf.gpuReason`, `perf.gpuFallback`; each render reports its own reason — `lcc_gpu::try_render`, `gpuFallback` per exported file — issue #675); short submissions for slow iGPUs; backend choice (DX12 only on Windows, `<PREFIX>_GPU_BACKEND` / `WGPU_BACKEND`; on Windows Settings ▸ Performance ▸ Graphics interface saves DX12 or Vulkan for GPU rendering, for machines where DX12's FXC shader build makes the start slow), device created after the window opens, crash sentinel (issue #136); a GPU lost while the app runs (driver reset) stops it with that message and turns GPU rendering off for the next start, which says why (issue #1337; the window's renderer can't survive the lost device); quitting waits (up to 2 s) for renders in flight and starts no new GPU work, so none is inside the driver when the process exits (`crates/gpu/src/exit.rs`, issue #620; seen crashing only on Linux + NVIDIA, other drivers unverified); renders wait for device memory instead of failing, and out of memory never turns the GPU off (upstream #1848); a lost device is not polled again and a poll that panics fails only that render (upstream #1984); a lost render device (or a fatal render error) no longer keeps renders on the CPU for the session: they go to the CPU while a new device is made on a thread of its own after 3 s, 6 s, 12 s … (at most five times), buffers and cached stages carry the device generation, and `app.gpu` tells each step in plain words (`events`, `recoveries`; `crates/gpu/src/slot.rs`, `tests/recovery.rs`, OneSlips' port); exports take turns at the GPU sized by its memory (55 % of what `lcc-gpumem` reports, at most 4 at once) behind the window's renders, a readback waits for its own submission and a timeout sends only that photo to the CPU (`crates/gpu/src/gate.rs`, `app.gpu` `batch`); no tiling beyond the buffer limit (CPU) GPU events (stopped responding, restarted, gave up) are shown as toasts; a window resize holds batch GPU work for a moment (`gpu::hold_batch`; OneSlips); the desktop app renders on the window's device and draws a drag's drafts straight from GPU textures (`lcc_gpu::try_render_display`, `<PREFIX>_GPU_WINDOW=0` opts out, `perf.mainOnGpu`); a second graphics card takes batch work and thumbnails at its own gate, and its loss leaves the editing card alone (`crates/gpu/src/helper.rs`, `tests/helper.rs`, verified with two devices on one card; OneSlips). |
| LR-BEHAV-RENDER-FIDELITY | Rendering matches Lightroom | P1 | 🟡 | `crates/pipeline/src/lib.rs`, `crates/pipeline/src/tone.rs` | every slider exists and works, but the character of the result (tone curve shape, highlight recovery, texture / clarity / dehaze, noise reduction, sharpening, default look) is tuned by eye; there is no systematic side-by-side comparison against Lightroom on the same CC0 raws. Needs a fidelity suite: Lightroom reference renders kept only in the local `plan/` (never committed), compared per slider and preset with a perceptual metric. Tuning that changes how existing settings render ships as a new process version (LRC-DEV-PROCESS, `docs/process-versions.md`). Camera colour and profile looks: `<cli> calibrate-lightroom` fits them black-box to Lightroom exports of contributors' own photos and reports held-out ΔE00 ([calibrate-lightroom](calibrate-lightroom.md)), and with `--editor` to Capture One, darktable, RawTherapee or DxO PhotoLab exports (`lcc_camdb::calibration::Editor`: a file per camera and editor, looks pooled per camera, only Lightroom's giving the default); no camera calibrated yet. `<cli> compare REFERENCE TEST` registers two renders from any editor and reports ΔE00 by tone band and hue with lightness / chroma / hue bias and a ΔE00 map (`crates/colourfit/src/compare.rs`): the suite's metric. `<cli> calibrate-lightroom verify --raws … --exports …` renders edited raws the way the app imports them (sidecar or the export's embedded `crs:`) and reports ΔE00 per photo and per group of controls used (`crates/colourfit/src/verify.rs`); no results recorded yet. `calibrate-lightroom probe` writes synthetic probe DNGs (384 patches through camera RGB) and one sweep step per Lightroom slider value; `probe-check` reads Lightroom's exports back by position and finds the app value whose change matches each Lightroom step (`crates/colourfit/src/probe.rs`) |
| LR-BEHAV-DRAGDROP | Drag and drop | P1 | ✅ | `crates/ui-egui/src/lib.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/grid.rs` (`drag_feedback`), `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/left.rs` (`drop_target`, `album_drag_over`, `top_level_drop_target`, `drag_auto_scroll`) | files → app (a drop-target card while they hover says what dropping does; photo files are added at once, a folder opens the import review, and a drop while an import runs is refused); grid photos → an album row (adds the selection, with a count badge while dragging); sidebar album / folder rows → a folder (moves it; its name follows the pointer); a drag of albums or grid photos near the top or bottom edge of the sidebar scrolls it (faster nearer the edge, only while the pointer is over the sidebar) |
| LR-BEHAV-TOAST | Toast notifications | P1 | ✅ | `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/mod.rs` | |
| LR-BEHAV-DIALOGS | Dialogs fit the window | P1 | ✅ | `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/dialogs.rs` (`dialog_body`, `list_height`), `crates/ui-egui/src/tests_dialog_fit.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/tests_dialogs.rs` (`a_click_outside_closes_the_dialog_when_settings_allow_it`) | a dialog taller than the window (Export on a 1366×768 screen, issue #781) never runs off it: its options scroll while the title bar and the Cancel / OK row stay on screen; lists that scroll on their own (What's New, All Metadata, shortcuts, smart-album rules, credits) get shorter first; dialogs that fit look as before; Import keeps its own resizable, scrolling window; Settings → Dialogs → Close a dialog by clicking outside it (off by default, so a stray click never throws away a dialog's choices): a click pressed and released on the dimmed backdrop closes the dialog like Esc and doesn't reach the panels below, while a drag that ends outside (a slider pulled past the edge) or a click that closes a dropdown keeps it; the import review never closes this way (`clicked_backdrop`) |
| LR-BEHAV-SIDEBAR-COLLAPSE | Collapsible left-sidebar sections | P2 | ✅ | `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/left.rs` (`section`, `section_header`), `crates/ui-egui/src/sidebar.rs` (`SidebarLayout`), `crates/ui-egui/src/tests_panels.rs` | click the Albums, Local, By Date, Folders or Keywords header (or drive `ui.clickWidget` `sidebarSection:<albums\|local\|byDate\|folders\|keywords>`) to fold or unfold the section; a chevron before the title shows the state; the choice is kept across restarts (`sidebar` in the UI state; the `collapsedSidebar` list of older settings files is still read). the click itself is UI-only, like the By Date / Keywords disclosure triangles, and `cmd:view.sidebarSection` `{section, collapsed}` folds and unfolds for agents; photos dropped on a folded Albums section have no target |
| LR-BEHAV-SIDEBAR-SECTIONS | Left-sidebar sections share one frame and one arrangement | P2 | ✅ | `crates/ui-egui/src/sidebar.rs` (`SidebarSection`, `SidebarLayout`), `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/left.rs` (`section`, `section_header`, `tree_toggle`), `crates/ui-egui/src/tests_panels.rs` | Albums, Local, By Date, Folders and Keywords are drawn by one `section` frame (the same gap above, header, fold and accessibility name) from one arrangement, `sidebar` in the UI state: a list of `{id, hidden, collapsed}` top to bottom, also settable with `ui.set`. Whatever is read, every section is in it exactly once (unknown and repeated entries are dropped, the rest follow in the usual order), so a damaged or newer settings file never loses the other settings. Rows that open (album folders, Local folders, dates, library folders, keywords) share `tree_toggle`: the same triangle, the same remembered open state, announced as Expand / Collapse. A section with nothing to list (Folders before anything is imported, Keywords before any is added) stays, open, with a one-line note in place of rows (`sidebarEmpty:<id>`) instead of disappearing. `ui.set` reads `sidebar` strictly (`SidebarLayout::parse`): an unknown or repeated section, or a flag that is not true / false, is refused and changes nothing; sections left out follow in the usual order, shown and open; the older `collapsedSidebar` list still folds and unfolds. A section may have one button at the end of its header, declared in one place (`header_button`: an icon, a tooltip, and either an action or a menu): the frame draws it at the visible edge in the header and its pinned copy, keeps its click from folding the section and leaves it room beside a folded section's count. Albums has + (`icon:albumNew`: Create Album / Smart Album / Folder…), Keywords has + (`icon:keywordNew`: Create Keyword Tag, for a keyword inside the default parent, not inside what the Keyword List has picked; once made, Keywords is shown and opened down to it). My Photos (All Photos, Recently Added, Picks, Missing Photos, Recently Deleted) is not a section: it stays on top. Since Develop has its own Classic left column (`classic_left.rs`), the sections are one panel of it — upstream's My Photos sidebar (`PanelId::Sources`), hidden until it is asked for and drawn by its own list rather than under a Classic header, the arrangement and the mechanics below being unchanged |
| LR-BEHAV-SIDEBAR-HIDE | Show and hide left-sidebar sections | P2 | ✅ | `cmd:view.sidebarSection`, `cmd:view.sidebarReset`, `crates/ui-egui/src/sidebar.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/left.rs` (`sections_menu`), `crates/ui-egui/src/menubar.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/tests_panels.rs` | Window ▸ Sidebar Sections lists Albums, Local, By Date, Folders and Keywords in the sidebar's order, each checked while shown; unchecking one takes its header and rows off the sidebar, checking it puts it back where and how it was (still folded if it was). The same list opens with a right-click on any section header (with Hide This Section first; widgets `sidebarMenu:hide`, `sidebarMenu:section:<id>`, `sidebarMenu:reset`) and on the My Photos title (`sidebarTitle`), which is always there, so hidden sections can always come back. Reset Sidebar Sections (enabled once something changed) shows and opens every section in the usual order. `view.sidebarSection` `{section, show?, collapsed?}` flips shown / hidden when neither flag is given and refuses an unknown section or a flag that is not true / false. Hiding the section of what the grid shows (an album, a keyword filter) leaves the grid as it is; an album made while Albums is hidden or folded brings Albums back, open. Kept with the UI state (`sidebar`). My Photos itself can't be hidden |
| LR-BEHAV-SIDEBAR-REORDER | Reorder left-sidebar sections | P2 | ✅ | `cmd:view.sidebarMoveSection`, `crates/ui-egui/src/sidebar.rs` (`move_before`, `move_by`), `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/left.rs` (`section_drag`, `section_drop_before`, `section_drag_feedback`), `crates/ui-egui/src/tests_panels.rs` | drag a section by its header: its name follows the pointer, a line (`sidebarSectionDrop`) shows the gap it would go to (above the section whose upper half the pointer is over, or below the last), releasing over the sidebar moves it there, Esc or releasing elsewhere leaves it; the sidebar scrolls when the drag nears its top or bottom edge, also over a pinned header (a section picked up by the pinned header scrolls once the pointer has left it). A press on a header's button is never a section drag. A click on the header still folds the section, and an album dragged onto the Albums header is still an album drag. The header's menu has Move Up / Move Down (`sidebarMenu:moveUp` / `moveDown`), one shown section at a time, stepping over hidden ones, disabled at the ends. `view.sidebarMoveSection` `{section, direction: up\|down}` or `{section, before: <section>\|null}` (null: last) answers `{moved, order}`. The order is kept with the UI state (`sidebar`). Recently Deleted is listed with My Photos (below Picks / Missing Photos), not after the sections. Not here: dragging My Photos, or rows from one section into another |
| LR-BEHAV-SIDEBAR-STICKY | Pinned section header and section look | P2 | ✅ | `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/left.rs` (`pinned_header`, `section_header`, `empty_hint`), `crates/ui-egui/src/tests_panels.rs` | while the rows of an open section are in view but its header has scrolled away, a copy of the header (`sidebarSectionPinned:<id>`, with the section's buttons and menu) stays at the top of the sidebar; the next section's header pushes it out; folding from it brings the section's own header into view. Rows scrolled beneath the pinned header are covered by it: an album or photos dropped on the pinned Albums header go to the top level / nowhere, never to the row under it; folding from it keeps the sideways scroll. Sections are told apart by a hairline in the gap above each; the fold chevron sits in the gutter, in the column of the rows' triangles (`sidebarSectionChevron:<id>`); a header highlights under the pointer; a folded section shows how many top-level rows it holds (`sidebarSectionCount:<id>`, with View ▸ Photo Counts); a count of 0 is fainter. Not here: a height of its own per section (one scroll area serves the whole sidebar), a count for Recently Added, Local's Browse Folder… as a header button |

The top bar has an Import button beside Export in every library. shared upstream gaps (filmstrip cells, keyword list, Quick Develop steppers, top-bar search) are listed in `docs/accessibility.md`; the crop frame and Remove spots move with the arrow keys (⌥ arrows resize the crop; `crates/ui-egui/src/tests_crop_tool.rs`); canvas tools by keyboard (`canvas_keys.rs`): crop corners, edges and box, spot targets and sources, a New spot point (Return adds), radial / linear gradient handles and mask pins take the focus, are announced with their position and move with the arrows (Shift: further), and Tab walks the right panels top to bottom; dialogs with one name field (New Album, New Smart Album, Save Metadata Preset, the renames) take the focus once when they open, so Tab and Shift+Tab reach their check box, Cancel and OK, and Return and Esc still confirm and cancel (`crates/ui-egui/src/tests_dialog_tab.rs`); Adding a language registers the locale and its language command, plus two catalogs. Stock presets/profiles, history steps, library headings, date headings and capture times are localised (user-named items stay verbatim); the Ukrainian catalog covers every existing catalog key, command/control label and rule-editor label; every catalog carries every message (a test fails on a message missing from any language, and on text in the Map, Book, Slideshow, Print, Web, tethering, publish, Immich, plug-in and Edit In UI that a catalog lacks — P6.3). Technical errors remain English; release notes are translated in German and Ukrainian; craft-fonts faces for other scripts (Thai, Arabic, …) are UI fallbacks in every language, for file, folder and album names (#736); Traditional Chinese borrows the Simplified Chinese face (no TC face in craft-fonts yet), and the web build embeds the Japanese face in WASM while loading the Simplified Chinese face from a separate font file. |

| LR-BEHAV-TITLEBAR | Title bar drag and double-click zoom (macOS) | P2 | 🟡 | `crates/ui-egui/src/titlebar.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/topbar.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/tests_titlebar.rs` | the native title bar is hidden on macOS, so the top bar's empty space (including the traffic-light margin) moves the window and a double-click zooms or restores it; buttons and the search field keep their clicks; ignored in full screen, where the traffic lights are gone and the bar's left buttons move into their space (#1763). Zoom is winit's `Maximized`: it does not follow System Settings › “Double-click a window's title bar to” (Fill / Minimize). a press that starts on a button or the search field never moves the window. Known gap: egui-winit reads the zoomed state only at start-up and after our own `Maximized` command, so a window zoomed another way (green button, Window ▸ Zoom, snapping) makes the first double-click a no-op before the toggle catches up. Checked by hand on a real macOS window (drag, zoom, restore) |

## Y. Menus

From `04-menu-tree.md`. ✅ = a command exists and is reachable from the UI (button, panel or shortcut). There is no
visible menu bar yet: the menu model is only exposed through the control channel (`ui.menu.list`).

| MENU-HELP-UPDATES | Check for updates | P2 | ✅ | `cmd:app.checkForUpdates`, `cmd:update.status`, `cmd:update.check`, `cmd:update.download`, `cmd:update.install`, `cmd:update.prefs`, `crates/updater`, `crates/engine/src/update.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/update.rs` | an extra (the reference app updates through its own service): Help ▸ Check for Updates… reads the release feed (the `latest.json` the release publishes, else GitHub's Releases API; only files on the project's own GitHub releases, over https, are offered), compares versions, shows the notes and what installing will do, downloads the asset for the platform (resumable; only once the release's `SHA256SUMS.txt` carries a valid signature from the key pinned in `brand.toml`, and checked against its size and that signed list's SHA-256; hashed again right before it is installed; a build without a key checks and offers the release page (`cmd:app.openReleasePage`) but installs nothing; in the activity stack) and installs it — the Windows MSI runs, the macOS disk image is mounted and its app (by name, version-checked) swapped in for this bundle with the old one put back on any failure (a build outside an `.app` is pointed to the release page), an AppImage replaces itself once the app has quit (a script waits up to 30 s for it, renames the new file over the old one in one step and starts it; the previous file, kept as `.old`, is deleted at the next start); a Windows copy unpacked from the portable zip (any copy Windows Installer says no MSI installed) unpacks the new zip beside itself, renames its own files aside under a locked manifest, moves the new ones in and starts the new version, which waits on a pipe until the old process has ended (all put back if a step or the start fails); the `.update-old` files go once the new version is up, and a new version that never came up is undone at its next start; a tarball, or a zip under Program Files or in a folder the copy can't write, is laid out for a manual install. The start-up check is opt-in (off by default; Settings ▸ General, the welcome dialog or the update dialog turn it on through `update.prefs`, and headless, `--memory` and `{env}_NO_PREFS` runs never check at start): one check then runs a few seconds after start-up; a release already skipped is not offered again |

| MENU-CTX-PROFILE | Profile favourites | P2 | ✅ | `cmd:profile.favorite`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/profiles.rs` | star in the profile browser; Favorites group first |

From `06-shortcuts.md` part 1. Evidence is our binding; conflicts are explained in
[UI parity → Shortcuts](ui-parity.md#shortcuts).

With a tool on the photo (Masking, Remove, Red Eye, the white-balance / colour-range / point-colour pickers, a targeted adjustment) held Space is the view's hand until the key is up, as in Lightroom: a click zooms to the click-zoom ratio at the point clicked (or back to Fit), a drag pans the zoomed photo (grab cursor; zoom-in cursor at Fit), and the tool gets neither; a tap (under 0.4 s, no click) toggles the zoom on its release (issue #1895) |
